# Tag <span class="tag big">method</span>

**A new way to do a task**: a method, system, algorithm, tool, prompt or training recipe that the item presents, whether or not it evaluates it (a design paper, a demo or a tool's manual counts).

<!-- filter -->

## Papers

- **[Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.13264 "Deep Reinforcement Learning at the Edge of the Statistical Precipice (2021)")** ((rliable), NeurIPS 2021). Argues that comparisons of deep RL methods from a handful of runs per task must report uncertainty: stratified bootstrap confidence intervals, performance profiles, and the interquartile mean (IQM) as aggregate (Tab. 1; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/agarwal2021rliable "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2108.13264) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.13264) · [Code](https://github.com/google-research/rliable)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[FIRE: Failure-Informed Runtime Engineering for Reliable Language-Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26048 "FIRE: Failure-Informed Runtime Engineering for Reliable Language-Model Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Runtime policies for a terminal coding agent (the Codex CLI on Terminal-Bench 2.1): short natural-language instructions or action denials that the harness applies when a trajectory reaches a state that preceded observed failures, with model weights and the user prompt unchanged (abstract; §3; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/agarwal2026fire "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26048)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a></span>
- **[GEPA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.19457 "GEPA: Reflective Prompt Evolution Can Outperform Reinforcement Learning (2026)")**: Reflective Prompt Evolution Can Outperform Reinforcement Learning (ICLR 2026). Reflective prompt evolution with Pareto selection. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/agrawal2025gepa "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.19457) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19457) · [Code: gepa](https://github.com/gepa-ai/gepa) · [Code: gepa-ai](https://github.com/gepa-ai/dspy) · [Code: gepa-artifact](https://github.com/gepa-ai/gepa-artifact)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[A State-of-the-Art SQL Reasoning Model using RLVR](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.21459 "A State-of-the-Art SQL Reasoning Model using RLVR (2025)")** ((Databricks), preprint 2025). A simple RLVR recipe for BIRD: prompt and model selection (GEPA mentioned), offline RL warm-up, then online RLVR. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ali2025sqlrlvr "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.21459) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21459)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[Instruction Retrieval at Inference Time for Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.13935 "Instruction Retrieval at Inference Time for Small Language Models (2025)")** (preprint 2025 (v3, 2026)). A teacher (GPT-5) writes one instruction per cluster of a domain's training questions, with background knowledge, a procedure and the common mistakes; a frozen small model retrieves the five instructions nearest its question and follows them, with no fine-tuning and no teacher call at inference (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/alkiek2025instructionretrieval "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.13935) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13935)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[Round-Trip Correctness (RTC)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08699 "Unsupervised Evaluation of Code LLMs with Round-Trip Correctness (2024)")**: Unsupervised Evaluation of Code LLMs with Round-Trip Correctness (ICML 2024). Evaluates code LLMs without human labels: describe code in natural language, synthesize code back, and check whether the result is semantically equivalent to the original (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/allamanis2024roundtrip "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.08699) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08699) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/icml2024-roundtrip-correctness)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a></span>
- **[Disproving Program Equivalence with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18473 "Disproving Program Equivalence with LLMs (2025)")** (preprint 2025). A white-box search for counterexamples to the equivalence of two or more executable programs, by an LLM with execution feedback (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/allamanis2025disproving "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.18473) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18473)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[ChipMEM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.27067 "ChipMEM: Verification-Grounded Memory for EDA Agents (2026)")**: Verification-Grounded Memory for EDA Agents (preprint 2026). A memory layer for LLM agents that optimize RTL hardware designs or write testbenches: skills retrieved by task similarity, plus Bayesian (Beta) estimates of tool-call success that advise on retries (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/alrabah2026chipmem "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.27067) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27067) · [Code](https://github.com/aalrabah/ChipMEM)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[The Stochastic Shift](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21133 "The Stochastic Shift: A New Evaluation Paradigm for Text-to-SQL with AI Operators (2026)")**: A New Evaluation Paradigm for Text-to-SQL with AI Operators (preprint 2026). Evaluates text-to-SQL whose queries call AI operators, on BigQuery and ThalamusDB (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/alrashed2026stochastic "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21133) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21133) · [Code](https://figshare.com/s/c75141469fb6dc74b890)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nondet-eval">nondet-eval</a></span>

- **[EvoSkill](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02766 "EvoSkill: Automated Skill Discovery for Multi-Agent Systems (2026)")**: Automated Skill Discovery for Multi-Agent Systems (preprint 2026). Grows a coding agent's library of skill folders (instructions, trigger metadata, helper scripts) from the agent's failures on labeled training questions, with the model frozen (abstract; §2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/alzubi2026evoskill "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.02766) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02766) · [Code](https://github.com/sentient-agi/EvoSkill)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29493 "Faults in Our Formal Benchmarking: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (2026)")**: Dataset Defects and Evaluation Failures in Lean Theorem Proving (ICML 2026). Audits five Lean theorem-proving benchmarks (miniF2F, ProofNet, FormalMath, CombiBench, ProverBench) and their forks, with a fault taxonomy: fidelity failures in formalization, evaluation-time loopholes, and maintenance decay (abstract; §1; Tab. 1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ammanamanchi2026leandefects "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29493) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29493) · [Code](https://github.com/Shashi456/atp-checkers)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Faithful Autoformalization via Roundtrip Verification and Repair](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25031 "Faithful Autoformalization via Roundtrip Verification and Repair (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Checks an LLM's translation of a natural-language rule into logic with no reference translation: formalize, translate back to English, formalize again, and ask an SMT solver whether the two formalizations are equivalent; when they are not, an LLM judge names the first translation step that lost meaning and only that step is repaired (abstract; §3–§4, Alg. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/amrollahi2026roundtrip "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.25031) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25031) · [Code](https://github.com/daneshvar-amrollahi/roundtrip-autoformalization)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[claimcheck: Measuring and Improving Specification Faithfulness](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17358 "claimcheck: Measuring and Improving Specification Faithfulness (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (PDF p. 1 footer: NeurIPS 2026 Workshop on AI for Verifiable Coding)). Checks whether a formal artifact means what its English requirement asks, a question no verifier asks: one model translates the artifact into English without seeing the requirement, and a second model, the judge, compares that text with the requirement (abstract; §3, Fig. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/amster2026claimcheck "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17358) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17358) · [Code](https://github.com/metareflection/claimcheck)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a></span>
- **[SQLFlex](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.16155 "Dialect-Agnostic SQL Parsing via LLM-Based Segmentation (2026)")**: Dialect-Agnostic SQL Parsing via LLM-Based Segmentation (PACMMOD 4(3) / SIGMOD 2026). Dialect-agnostic parsing: grammar-based parsing plus LLM segmentation with validation checks. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/an2026parsing "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.16155) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16155) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3802038)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/reduce">reduce</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-parse">dialect-parse</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[Attention Tax, Handoff Tax](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.06069 "Attention Tax, Handoff Tax: A Stylised Model of When Multi-Agent LLM Systems Help (2026)")**: A Stylised Model of When Multi-Agent LLM Systems Help (preprint 2026). A stylised reliability model of single- against multi-agent LLM systems: per-step failure rises with the context an agent attends over, handing work to a fresh agent costs a "handoff tax" per boundary, and parallel samples share part of their failures (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/anchan2026handofftax "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.06069) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06069) · [Code](https://github.com/akshitanchan/attention-handoff-tax)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/theory">theory</a></span>
- **[Memory Reward Inflation in Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.00017 "Memory Reward Inflation in Self-Improving LLM Agents (2026)")** ((Echo Gap, LUCID), preprint 2026). A memory agent stores each episode with a score it gives itself and reuses high-scored ones; the authors call the inflated scores of wrong episodes the "Echo Gap", argue that reuse compounds it, and state an Error-Independence Assumption (EIA): a correcting signal must track truth and have errors uncorrelated with the self-grade's (abstract; §1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/asadolahi2026memoryinflation "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.00017) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00017)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

Human-centered rewriting middleware: users write rules (VarSQL) or give example pairs it generalizes. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bai2023querybooster "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.08272) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.08272) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3611479.3611497) · [Code](https://github.com/ISG-ICS/QueryBooster)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>

A community platform for contributing and sharing rewrite rules. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bai2026sqlrewriter "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/icde65706.2026.00326)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[DISCERN ("Pay Only for Disagreement")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.17560 "Pay Only for Disagreement: Certified No-Regression Verdicts for Model Updates with Matching Label-Complexity Bounds (2026)")**: Pay Only for Disagreement: Certified No-Regression Verdicts for Model Updates with Matching Label-Complexity Bounds (preprint 2026). Certifies whether a model update (retrain, fine-tune, quantization, vendor swap) is a regression: a sequential audit of the paired risk difference between old and new model, with anytime-valid confidence sequences, valid under any predictable label-routing rule (abstract; §4; §5.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/balachandran2026discern "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.17560) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17560)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a></span>
- **[Apache Calcite](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1802.10233 "Apache Calcite: A Foundational Framework for Optimized Query Processing Over Heterogeneous Data Sources (2018)")**: A Foundational Framework for Optimized Query Processing Over Heterogeneous Data Sources (SIGMOD 2018). A framework with parsing, relational algebra, many optimization rules and a Volcano-style planner. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/begoli2018calcite "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1802.10233) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.10233) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3183713.3190662) · [Code](https://github.com/apache/calcite)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairgen-apply">pairgen-apply</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[What Fits (Into Few Tokens) Doesn't Overfit: Compression and Generalization in ML Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11045 "What Fits (Into Few Tokens) Doesn't Overfit: Compression and Generalization in ML Research Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Asks why reusing a validation set adaptively causes little overfitting, and tests the hypothesis that successful strategies are compressible on LLM research agents (Claude Opus in Claude Code) that edit and train models on 8 tasks, from tabular classification to language and diffusion modeling, scored on a separate holdout (abstract; §4, Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bertran2026compression "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11045) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11045)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>

A text-to-SQL benchmark where each question has two plausible SQL readings, from lexical or structural ambiguity (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bhaskar2023ambiqt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.13659) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13659) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.436) · [Code](https://github.com/testzer0/AmbiQT)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[RLMT ("Language Models that Think](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.20357 "Language Models that Think, Chat Better (2026)")**: Language Models that Think, Chat Better (Chat Better"), COLM 2026). Trains 7–8B models to write a long chain of thought before each chat response, rewarded by a learned preference reward model as in RLHF instead of a verifier, and pairs each run with a matched non-thinking RLHF run (abstract; §2.2, §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bhaskar2025rlmt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.20357) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20357) · [Code](https://github.com/princeton-pli/RLMT)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[interwhen](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11202 "interwhen: A Generalizable Framework for Steering Reasoning Models with Test-time Verification (2026)")**: A Generalizable Framework for Steering Reasoning Models with Test-time Verification (preprint 2026). Steers a single reasoning or agent trajectory with verifiers that run alongside generation: a forked copy of the model (on τ²-bench and VitaBench, a separate Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct, §5) periodically extracts verifiable states from the partial trace, and a violation goes back to the model as feedback (§1; §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bhat2026interwhen "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11202) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11202) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/interwhen)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[RoboPhD](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.01126 "RoboPhD: Self-Improving Text-to-SQL Through Autonomous Agent Evolution (2026)")**: Self-Improving Text-to-SQL Through Autonomous Agent Evolution (preprint 2026). An evolution agent rewrites a text-to-SQL agent (a database-analysis script and SQL-generation instructions) from performance feedback, with ELO-based selection (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/borthwick2026robophd "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.01126) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01126) · [Code](https://github.com/andborth/RoboPhD)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[What Stops a Small Language Model From Driving a Database Agent](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21341 "What Stops a Small Language Model From Driving a Database Agent (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Drives the agent mode of LibreDB Studio, an open-source SQL client, with open-weight models served locally and one hosted control, across six task surfaces (abstract; §3; Tab. 1); the authors are contributors to the software evaluated (p. 1; § Conflict of interest). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/bozoglan2026slmdbagent "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21341) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21341) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.57967/hf/10485)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Large Language Models as Tool Makers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.17126 "Large Language Models as Tool Makers (2024)")** (ICLR 2024). A strong model writes a reusable Python function (a tool) for a class of tasks; a lighter model solves each instance by calling it (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cai2023toolmakers "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.17126) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17126) · [Code](https://github.com/ctlllll/LLM-ToolMaker)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[MultiPL-T](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.09895 "Knowledge Transfer from High-Resource to Low-Resource Programming Languages for Code LLMs (2024)")**: Knowledge Transfer from High-Resource to Low-Resource Programming Languages for Code LLMs (OOPSLA 2024 (PACMPL 8, OOPSLA2)). Makes fine-tuning data for programming languages code LLMs are weak in (Julia, Lua, OCaml, R, Racket): a code LLM translates documented Python functions from The Stack, and only translations that pass the function's LLM-written, execution-checked Python tests, compiled into the target language without an LLM, are kept (abstract; §4, Fig. 6). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cassano2023multiplt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.09895) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.09895) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3689735) · [Code](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.12453932)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[EvoSQL](#/papers/castelein2018evosql "Search-Based Test Data Generation for SQL Queries (2018)")**: Search-Based Test Data Generation for SQL Queries (ICSE 2018). Generates test data covering each SQLFpc coverage target of a query. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/castelein2018evosql "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3180155.3180202) · [Code: evosql](https://github.com/SERG-Delft/evosql) · [Code: evosql-appendix](https://zenodo.org/records/1166023)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[XData](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1411.6704 "Data generation for testing and grading SQL queries (2015)")**: Data generation for testing and grading SQL queries (VLDB J. 24(6) 2015). Generates small datasets that "kill" query mutants (join-type, comparison, aggregation, …). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chandra2014xdata "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1411.6704) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.6704) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00778-015-0395-0)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[Edit Based Grading of SQL Queries](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.09019 "Edit Based Grading of SQL Queries (2019)")** (arXiv 2019, published at CODS-COMAD 2021; a short version is ICDE 2019 ("Automated Grading of SQL Queries")). Grades student SQL: XData's generated test databases decide correctness, and partial marks come from an edit distance to the nearest correct query. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chandra2019grading "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.09019) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.09019)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[Naive Prompt Optimization (NPO)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27266 "Naive Prompt Optimization: Rethinking the Need for Complex Prompt Search (2026)")**: Naive Prompt Optimization: Rethinking the Need for Complex Prompt Search (preprint 2026). A single lineage of prompts revised by a teacher model from rollout feedback. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chang2026npo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27266) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27266)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Multi-modal Synthesis of Regular Expressions](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1908.03316 "Multi-modal Synthesis of Regular Expressions (2020)")** (PLDI 2020). Synthesizes a regular expression from an English description together with positive and negative example strings: a semantic parser turns the English into a ranked list of hierarchical sketches (partial regexes whose holes carry hints), and a programming-by-example engine completes each sketch into a regex consistent with the examples (abstract; §1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2019regel "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1908.03316) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.03316) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3385412.3385988)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-regex">dsl-regex</a></span>
- **[CodeT](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.10397 "CodeT: Code Generation with Generated Tests (2023)")**: Code Generation with Generated Tests (ICLR 2023). The same code model that samples candidate programs is prompted to write test cases (input and expected output) for the problem; every program runs on every test, programs that pass the same tests form a consensus set, and the set scored by its numbers of programs and tests supplies the answer ("dual execution agreement", RANSAC-inspired) (abstract; §2.1–2.2). Zero-shot on HumanEval, MBPP, APPS and CodeContests with three Codex models, InCoder-6B and CodeGen-16B (§3); APPS and CodeContests with code-davinci-002 only (§4.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2022codet "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.10397) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.10397) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeT)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[PoT (Program of Thoughts prompting)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.12588 "Program of Thoughts Prompting: Disentangling Computation from Reasoning for Numerical Reasoning Tasks (2023)")**: Program of Thoughts Prompting: Disentangling Computation from Reasoning for Numerical Reasoning Tasks (TMLR 2023). Prompts an LLM (mainly Codex) to write its reasoning as a Python program with meaningful variable names; a Python interpreter, with SymPy for equations, runs it and its result is the answer, so the computation is taken out of the model's text (abstract, §2.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2022pot "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.12588) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.12588) · [Code](https://github.com/wenhuchen/Program-of-Thoughts)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Self-Debugging](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.05128 "Teaching Large Language Models to Self-Debug (2023)")**: Teaching Large Language Models to Self-Debug (preprint 2023 (published at ICLR 2024)). Few-shot demonstrations teach an LLM to debug its own program from execution results and explanations (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2023selfdebug "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.05128) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.05128)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[Steering Large Language Models between Code Execution and Textual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.03524 "Steering Large Language Models between Code Execution and Textual Reasoning (2025)")** (ICLR 2025). Measures when LLMs answer by writing and running code rather than in text, and how well 7 existing ways of steering that choice work (prompts asking for code or for text, AutoGen's system prompt, OpenAI's Code Interpreter), on 14 math, logic, robot-planning and symbolic tasks with 6 LLMs (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2024steering "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.03524) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03524) · [Code](https://github.com/yongchao98/CodeSteer-v1.0)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[GBV-SQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.12612 "GBV-SQL: Guided Generation and SQL2Text Back-Translation Validation for Multi-Agent Text2SQL (2025)")**: Guided Generation and SQL2Text Back-Translation Validation for Multi-Agent Text2SQL (preprint 2025 (the litsearch listed ACL 2025)). Multi-agent text-to-SQL that translates the generated SQL back into text and checks it against the question. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2025gbvsql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.12612) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.12612)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[ParSEval](https://dbgroup.cs.tsinghua.edu.cn/jnwang/papers/vldb2025-parseval.pdf "ParSEval: Plan-aware Test Database Generation for SQL Equivalence Evaluation (2025)")**: Plan-aware Test Database Generation for SQL Equivalence Evaluation (PVLDB 18(11) 2025). Generates test databases that aim to cover every branch of the queries' logical plans (abstract; a greedy heuristic, §4.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2025parseval "Summary") · [PDF](https://dbgroup.cs.tsinghua.edu.cn/jnwang/papers/vldb2025-parseval.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3749646.3749727) · [Code](https://github.com/sfu-db/ParSEval)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[Seed-Prover](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.23726 "Seed-Prover: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (2025)")**: Deep and Broad Reasoning for Automated Theorem Proving (preprint 2025). A lemma-style Lean whole-proof model that refines its proof from Lean feedback, proved lemmas and self-summaries (abstract), trained with RL (§2.2.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2025seedprover "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.23726) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23726) · [Code](https://github.com/ByteDance-Seed/Seed-Prover)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Adaptive-GEPA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.38762 "Adaptive-GEPA: Make Your Harness Fit Heterogeneous Requests (2026)")**: Make Your Harness Fit Heterogeneous Requests (preprint 2026). Evolves a router and a library of specialist programs under one search budget. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026adaptivegepa "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.38762) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38762)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Learning from Prompt itself: the Hierarchical Attribution Prompt Optimization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.02683 "Learning from Prompt itself: the Hierarchical Attribution Prompt Optimization (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A prompt optimizer that targets "prompt drift", where new prompts fix earlier failures but break earlier successes (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026attribution "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.02683) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02683)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[When Does Combining Language Models Help? A Co-Failure Ceiling on Routing, Voting, and Mixture-of-Agents Across 67 Frontier Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27288 "When Does Combining Language Models Help? A Co-Failure Ceiling on Routing, Voting, and Mixture-of-Agents Across 67 Frontier Models (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Any policy whose output is one member model's answer (a router, a vote, a cascade) has accuracy at most 1 − β, where β is the rate at which every model in the pool is wrong on the same query. The average pairwise error correlation ρ, the diagnostic practice reports, cannot identify β (abstract; §5, Props. 1 and 3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026cofailure "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27288) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27288)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Compilation Is Not Correctness: Scan-by-Scan Evaluation of LLM-Generated PLC Programs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28675 "Compilation Is Not Correctness: Scan-by-Scan Evaluation of LLM-Generated PLC Programs (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of 301 tasks for LLMs writing Structured Text (ST: a high-level textual language defined in IEC 61131-3 for programmable logic controllers, which run their logic in repeated scans), each with a reviewed public specification and a hidden reference program and input scenarios; a program passes only if it keeps the public interface, compiles, completes every scenario and matches the reference's outputs after every scan (abstract; §3). The authors call it "To our knowledge, the first benchmark for LLM-based Structured Text generation with comprehensive per-scan behavioral evaluation against hidden references" (§1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026cyclebench "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28675) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28675)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[EquiTrace](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30100 "Building SQL Equivalence Proofs from Query Rewrite Rules (2026)")**: Building SQL Equivalence Proofs from Query Rewrite Rules (preprint Oct 2026 (CIDR 2027 class file)). Proves two SQL queries equivalent by rewriting both, with admitted rewrite rules, to a common logical expression; the two derivations are the proof, returned as a replayable certificate, else UNKNOWN. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026equitrace "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30100) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30100)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/prove-memo">prove-memo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-eval">rewrite-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[Learning to Self-Evolve (LSE)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.18620 "Learning to Self-Evolve (2026)")**: Learning to Self-Evolve (preprint 2026). RL-trains a 4B model to edit its own context; each edit is rewarded by the downstream improvement. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026lse "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.18620) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18620) · [Code](https://github.com/chenyn66/learning-to-self-evolve)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Reason Popper-ly: Patching In-Context Reasoning with Inductive Logic Programming](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.23019 "Reason Popper-ly: Patching In-Context Reasoning with Inductive Logic Programming (2026)")** (accepted at the 20th Conference on Neurosymbolic Learning and Reasoning, 2026). Checks each inferred step of an LLM's chain of thought against a table of kinship-composition rules, classifies each violation into one of four error types, rewrites the wrong step and has the LLM regenerate the rest of the trace from the corrected prefix, then answer from the repaired trace (abstract; §3.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chen2026popper "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.23019) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23019)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a></span>
- **[BPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.04155 "Black-Box Prompt Optimization: Aligning Large Language Models without Model Training (2024)")**: Black-Box Prompt Optimization: Aligning Large Language Models without Model Training (ACL 2024). Trains a small model that rewrites user prompts so a black-box LLM answers better. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2023bpo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.04155) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.04155) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.176) · [Code](https://github.com/thu-coai/BPO)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>
- **[Trace / OptoPrime](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.16218 "Trace is the Next AutoDiff: Generative Optimization with Rich Feedback, Execution Traces, and LLMs (2024)")**: Trace is the Next AutoDiff: Generative Optimization with Rich Feedback, Execution Traces, and LLMs (NeurIPS 2024). Optimizes general workflows (prompts, code, hyperparameters) from rich feedback such as console output or a user's responses (abstract) and execution errors (§1.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2024trace "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.16218) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16218) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/Trace)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Revisiting Reinforcement Learning for LLM Reasoning from A Cross-Domain Perspective](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14965 "Revisiting Reinforcement Learning for LLM Reasoning from A Cross-Domain Perspective (2025)")** (NeurIPS 2025). A 92K-example RL corpus over six domains (Math, Code, Science, Logic, Simulation, Tabular), each with its own verifiable reward (abstract); the authors report that Math, Code and Science "easily benefit from cross-domain RL training", while Logic, Simulation and Tabular, which they describe as having "limited pretraining exposure", "require in-domain training to achieve meaningful performance gains" (abstract; §3.1, Fig. 3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2025guru "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14965) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14965) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/085713-4479) · [Code](https://github.com/LLM360/Reasoning360)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[Scope Before You Persist](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.29144 "Scope Before You Persist: Preventing Cross-Family Interference in Agent Memory (2026)")**: Preventing Cross-Family Interference in Agent Memory (preprint 2026). A frozen model edits a 360-character skill policy for a frozen actor round by round, and a gate (ORC: public examples, private tests and metamorphic checks, each with a paired-bootstrap bound, no regression on earlier families) decides each edit; the authors then retrieve each accepted skill only for the task family that produced it (§1; §3; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2026scope "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.29144) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29144)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[VeRA: Renewing Reasoning Benchmarks with Executable Specifications](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.13217 "VeRA: Renewing Reasoning Benchmarks with Executable Specifications (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2, a rewrite of v1)). Turns each item of a reasoning benchmark into an executable task family, a question template, an input generator and a deterministic answer program, so that new labelled instances come from running code; VeRA-E draws fresh instances of the same family, VeRA-H modifies the family toward harder tasks, and VeRA-H Pro keeps the judge-ranked best of up to five validated proposals per seed (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cheng2026vera "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.13217) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13217) · [Code](https://github.com/Marco-Cheng/VeRA)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a></span>
- **[HoTTSQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1607.04822 "HoTTSQL: proving query rewrites with univalent SQL semantics (2017)")**: proving query rewrites with univalent SQL semantics (PLDI 2017). Machine-checkable denotational SQL semantics in Coq: K-relations plus homotopy type theory, covering bags, correlated subqueries, aggregation. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chu2016hottsql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1607.04822) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.04822) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3062341.3062348) · [Code](https://github.com/uwdb/Cosette)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[Cosette](#/papers/chu2017cosette "Cosette: An Automated Prover for SQL (2017)")**: An Automated Prover for SQL (CIDR 2017). Automated SQL prover: a Rosette/SMT bounded search for counterexamples plus a Coq prover for equivalence. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chu2017cosette "Summary") · [Paper](https://www.cidrdb.org/cidr2017/papers/p51-chu-cidr17.pdf) · [Code](https://github.com/uwdb/Cosette)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/bounded">bounded</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[UDP](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1802.02229 "Axiomatic foundations and algorithms for deciding semantic equivalences of SQL queries (2018)")**: Axiomatic foundations and algorithms for deciding semantic equivalences of SQL queries (PVLDB 11(11) 2018). U-semirings: semiring semantics with unbounded summation and duplicate elimination, implemented in a proof assistant. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/chu2018udp "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1802.02229) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.02229) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3236187.3236200) · [Code](https://github.com/uwdb/Cosette)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[Better Policies, Worse Verifier Audits](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29029 "Better Policies, Worse Verifier Audits (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Shows that scoring a component verifier on fresh policy outputs against the full success label can stop measuring that component as the policy masters it ("on-policy audit collapse"), can prefer a worse verifier, and, when used to trigger a refresh, can make the verifier worse (abstract; §1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cieslak2026audits "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29029) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29029)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[XML Path Language (XPath) Version 1.0](#/papers/clark1999xpath "XML Path Language (XPath) Version 1.0 (1999)")** (W3C Recommendation 16 November 1999 (status updated October 2016); a standard, filed as a paper). Defines XPath 1.0, "a language for addressing parts of an XML document" made for XSLT and XPointer (abstract; §1), with a grammar in EBNF (§1), a tree data model of seven node types (§5) and a core function library every implementation must support (§1; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/clark1999xpath "Summary") · [Paper](https://www.w3.org/TR/1999/REC-xpath-19991116)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-path">dsl-path</a></span>
- **[GSM8K and trained verifiers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.14168 "Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems (2021)")**: Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems (preprint 2021). Introduces GSM8K and trains a verifier on final-answer correctness labels to rank sampled solutions (§4.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/cobbe2021verifiers "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.14168) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.14168) · [Code](https://github.com/openai/grade-school-math)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[SKILLER](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10538 "SKILLER: Language-Level Reinforcement Learning for Reusable Skill Extraction in Small Language Models (2026)")**: Language-Level Reinforcement Learning for Reusable Skill Extraction in Small Language Models (preprint 2026). A frontier model (GPT-5.4) acts as critic and actor to write and revise a task-specific agent skill for a frozen small model (Qwen3.5-9B or Qwen3.5-4B) running in the OpenCode agent loop, rewarded by each benchmark's official verifier; the authors call it reinforcement learning in natural language, since every signal is text and no weights change (abstract; § "Method", § "Experimental Setup"). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/dang2026skiller "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10538) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10538) · [Code](https://github.com/DANG-ai/SKILLER)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[DeepSeek-R1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948 "DeepSeek-R1 incentivizes reasoning in LLMs through reinforcement learning (2025)")**: DeepSeek-R1 incentivizes reasoning in LLMs through reinforcement learning (Nature 645 (2025)). Reasoning learned by RL with rule-based rewards (accuracy and format), starting without SFT (R1-Zero). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/deepseek2025reasoning "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12948) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-09422-z) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[A framework for assessing the capabilities of code generation of constraint domain-specific languages with large language models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.05278 "A framework for assessing the capabilities of code generation of constraint domain-specific languages with large language models (2026)")** (Alloy) with LLMs, Journal of Systems and Software 238 (2026), 112871). A configurable pipeline that turns natural-language constraint descriptions, plus a text description of the domain, a domain model or both, into code in the constraint languages OCL and Alloy, or in Python as a high-resource baseline, then checks each snippet for well-formedness and correctness, with an optional single round of LLM repair after each check (abstract; §3, Fig. 1–3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/delgado2026constraintdsl "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.05278) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05278) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2026.112871) · [Code](https://github.com/david-xander/code-generation-framework-for-dsls)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[LITHE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.12918 "LITHE: A Query Rewrite Advisor using LLMs (2026)")**: A Query Rewrite Advisor using LLMs (EDBT 2026). Prompt ensemble, database-sensitive prompts and LLM token-probability-guided rewrite paths (MCTS). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/dharwada2025lithe "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.12918) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12918) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.48786/edbt.2026.20)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[SQLSolver](https://www.cs.yale.edu/homes/piskac/papers/2024SIGMOD.pdf "Proving Query Equivalence Using Linear Integer Arithmetic (2023)")**: Proving Query Equivalence Using Linear Integer Arithmetic (PACMMOD 1(4) / SIGMOD 2024, 2023). Unbounded prover that handles U-expression *unbounded summations* via LIA\* (linear integer arithmetic with stars). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ding2023sqlsolver "Summary") · [PDF](https://www.cs.yale.edu/homes/piskac/papers/2024SIGMOD.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3626768) · [Code](https://github.com/SJTU-IPADS/SQLSolver)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[SemCoder](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.01006 "SemCoder: Training Code Language Models with Comprehensive Semantics Reasoning (2024)")**: Training Code Language Models with Comprehensive Semantics Reasoning (NeurIPS 2024). Fine-tunes DeepSeekCoder-6.7B jointly on writing Python from English and on "monologue reasoning", natural-language explanations of a function's purpose, the properties and constraints that always hold, and its execution, forward to the output and backward to a possible input; the authors report "competitive performance with GPT-3.5-turbo on code generation and execution reasoning tasks" (abstract; §6.1, Tab. 1), and that SemCoder-S, the variant also trained on extra instruction data, outperforms four open instruction-tuned models at iterative debugging and self-refinement (§6.3, Tab. 3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ding2024semcoder "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.01006) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01006) · [Code](https://github.com/ARiSE-Lab/SemCoder)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[SlabCity](#/papers/dong2023slabcity "SlabCity: Whole-Query Optimization Using Program Synthesis (2023)")**: Whole-Query Optimization Using Program Synthesis (PVLDB 16(11) 2023). Rule-free whole-query rewriting by program synthesis, with a new LeetCode benchmark with constraints. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/dong2023slabcity "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3611479.3611515) · [Code](https://github.com/eidos06/SlabCity)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-eval">rewrite-eval</a></span>
- **[STP](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.00212 "STP: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (2025)")**: Self-play LLM Theorem Provers with Iterative Conjecturing and Proving (ICML 2025). One model is both conjecturer and prover: conjectures barely provable by the current prover train the conjecturer, and the prover uses expert iteration (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/dong2025stp "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.00212) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00212) · [Code](https://github.com/kfdong/STP)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[XQuery 1.0 and XPath 2.0 Formal Semantics (Second Edition)](#/papers/draper2010semantics "XQuery 1.0 and XPath 2.0 Formal Semantics (Second Edition) (2010)")** (W3C Recommendation 2010, revised 2015 (a standard, not a paper)). W3C's formal semantics of XQuery 1.0 and XPath 2.0: each expression is rewritten ("normalized") into a smaller Core language, which gets static typing rules and dynamic evaluation rules, written as inference rules (§3.2; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/draper2010semantics "Summary") · [Paper](http://www.w3.org/TR/2010/REC-xquery-semantics-20101214/)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-path">dsl-path</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nondet-semantics">nondet-semantics</a></span>
- **[Context Length Alone Hurts LLM Performance Despite Perfect Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05381 "Context Length Alone Hurts LLM Performance Despite Perfect Retrieval (2025)")** (Findings of EMNLP 2025). Measures whether an LLM that can still recite all the evidence of a problem word for word solves it as well over a long input as over a short one: GSM8K, MMLU, HumanEval and a synthetic variable-summation task, each split into evidence and question with filler tokens inserted between them (§3.1, Tab. 1), on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct and Mistral-v0.3-7B-Instruct, and on three closed models in one setting (§3.2, §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/du2025contextlength "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05381) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05381)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/longctx">longctx</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Generalization in Adaptive Data Analysis and Holdout Reuse](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1506.02629 "Generalization in Adaptive Data Analysis and Holdout Reuse (2015)")** (NeurIPS 2015). Gives algorithms that let an analyst validate many adaptively chosen hypotheses against one holdout set without overfitting to it: Thresholdout answers with the training-set value unless it differs from the holdout value by more than a noisy threshold, so a hypothesis that does not overfit reveals little about the holdout set; SparseValidate answers arbitrary Boolean queries and is analysed by description length (abstract; §1.1; §4.1–4.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/dwork2015holdout "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1506.02629) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1506.02629)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a></span>
- **[DAEDALUS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.08048 "DAEDALUS: Bootstrapping Agent Memory from Self-Generated Tasks (2026)")**: Bootstrapping Agent Memory from Self-Generated Tasks (preprint 2026). Builds an agent's memory with no training tasks and no oracle verifier: an Explorer proposes tasks with success conditions and tunes their difficulty, a Solver attempts them, and a heuristic written from each failure is kept only after the Solver then succeeds with it several times in a row; the kept heuristics are merged into one bank given to the agent at test time (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/edy2026daedalus "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.08048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08048) · [Code](https://github.com/illuin-tech/daedalus)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Horizon](#/papers/emani2025horizon "Horizon: Robust Checks for SQL Migration Using LLMs (2025)")**: Robust Checks for SQL Migration Using LLMs (PVLDB 18(12) 2025 (demo)). Microsoft's LLM-based schema and code migration, with checks for syntactic completeness and functional equivalence. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/emani2025horizon "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3750601.3750646)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[anthem 2.0](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.11704 "anthem 2.0: Automated Reasoning for Answer Set Programming (2025)")**: Automated Reasoning for Answer Set Programming (Theory and Practice of Logic Programming 25(4) 2025 (ICLP 2025)). A tool that verifies answer set programs written in mini-gringo, a fragment of the clingo solver's input language with arithmetic and simple choice rules but no aggregates (abstract, §2): it proves strong equivalence (the same stable models inside any larger program), external equivalence (the same output for every input that a "user guide" permits) and adherence to a first-order specification (§4.1–4.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/fandinno2025anthem "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.11704) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11704) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1017/S1471068425100112) · [Code](https://github.com/potassco/anthem)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-datalog">dsl-datalog</a></span>
- **[ReSequel](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.20853 "ReSequel: Robust LLM-assisted Query Rewriting and Optimization using Templatization and Sampling (2026)")**: Robust LLM-assisted Query Rewriting and Optimization using Templatization and Sampling (PVLDB 19(10), 2026). An outer optimization layer that rewrites query templates with LLMs. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/fathollahzadeh2026resequel "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.20853) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20853) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3828612.3828639) · [Code](https://github.com/CoDS-GCS/ReSequel)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[ReTool](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.11536 "ReTool: Reinforcement Learning for Strategic Tool Use in LLMs (2026)")**: Reinforcement Learning for Strategic Tool Use in LLMs (ICLR 2026). Trains an LLM with reinforcement learning to interleave Python code, run in a sandbox, with its text reasoning on competition math, rewarded only on whether the final answer matches (abstract; §2.3.1, Eq. 2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/feng2025retool "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.11536) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11536) · [Code](https://github.com/ReTool-RL/ReTool)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[Promptbreeder](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.16797 "Promptbreeder: Self-Referential Self-Improvement Via Prompt Evolution (2023)")**: Self-Referential Self-Improvement Via Prompt Evolution (preprint, 2023). Evolves task prompts and the mutation prompts that change them. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/fernando2023promptbreeder "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.16797) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16797)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Baldur](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.04910 "Baldur: Whole-Proof Generation and Repair with Large Language Models (2023)")**: Whole-Proof Generation and Repair with Large Language Models (ESEC/FSE 2023). Fine-tuned LLMs generate whole Isabelle/HOL proofs at once, instead of one step at a time inside a search (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/first2023baldur "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.04910) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04910) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3611643.3616243)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Optimization of Nested SQL Queries Revisited](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/38713.38723 "Optimization of Nested SQL Queries Revisited (1987)")** (SIGMOD 1987). Shows that Kim's unnesting algorithm NEST-JA is wrong for COUNT over empty groups (the "COUNT bug", found by Kiessling in a 1984 memo, §5.1) and for join predicates other than equality (§5.3); the outer-join fix for the first then fails when the outer relation has duplicates in the join column (§5.4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ganski1987nested "Summary") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/38713.38723) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/38713.38723)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[PAL (Program-aided Language Models)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.10435 "PAL: Program-aided Language Models (2022)")**: PAL: Program-aided Language Models (preprint 2022). Few-shot prompts make the LLM write its reasoning steps as a Python program; a Python interpreter runs it and its result is the answer (abstract, §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/gao2022pal "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.10435) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.10435) · [Code](https://github.com/reasoning-machines/pal)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Externalising Reasoning by Teaching Language Models to Use Programs as Tools](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/86006/3/2026GemmellG%C3%B3rrizPhD.pdf "Externalising Reasoning by Teaching Language Models to Use Programs as Tools (2026)")** ((ToolWriter, GRILLBot), PhD thesis, University of Glasgow, 2026). A PhD thesis (work from 2019–2023, §1.4) arguing that language models can write programs and use them as tools "to externalise reasoning at scale" on semi-structured data (§1.5, PDF p. 23): English-to-Python generation (Ch. 4–5), question-specific row-filter programs for question answering over tables (ToolWriter, Ch. 6, from EMNLP 2023), and one Python call per turn as the policy of the GRILLBot voice assistant (Ch. 7) (§1.7, PDF p. 27). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/gemmellgorriz2026externalising "Summary") · [PDF](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/86006/3/2026GemmellG%C3%B3rrizPhD.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.5525/gla.thesis.86006)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a></span>
- **[Efficient Static Analysis of XML Paths and Types](https://inria.hal.science/inria-00502789/document "Efficient Static Analysis of XML Paths and Types (2007)")** (PLDI 2007). Decides XPath emptiness, containment, overlap and coverage, with or without regular tree type constraints (DTDs and other XML schemas), by testing whether one formula over finite trees is satisfiable (abstract, PDF p. 1; §8, PDF pp. 8–9); the XPath fragment "covers all features except counting [8] and data values" (§1, PDF p. 1), though its abstract syntax (Fig. 3, PDF p. 3) has 11 of XPath 1.0's 13 axes (no attribute or namespace axis). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/geneves2007efficient "Summary") · [PDF](https://inria.hal.science/inria-00502789/document) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/1250734.1250773)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-path">dsl-path</a></span>
- **[XML Static Analyzer User Manual](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0812.3550 "XML Static Analyzer User Manual (2008)")** (INRIA research report RR-6726 (arXiv 2008)). User manual of a satisfiability solver for a logic of finite trees that decides XPath emptiness, containment and equivalence, optionally under a DTD, XML Schema or Relax NG type, and outputs a satisfying or counterexample XML tree (§2, Examples 1–3; §3.2 "Supported XML Types"; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/geneves2008analyzer "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/0812.3550) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/0812.3550)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-path">dsl-path</a></span>
- **[The Volcano Optimizer Generator](#/papers/graefe1993volcano "The Volcano optimizer generator: extensibility and efficient search (1993)")**: The Volcano optimizer generator: extensibility and efficient search (ICDE 1993). An optimizer generator: transformation and implementation rules in, a memoizing top-down search out. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/graefe1993volcano "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/icde.1993.344061)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[The Cascades Framework for Query Optimization](#/papers/graefe1995cascades "The Cascades Framework for Query Optimization (1995)")** (IEEE Data Eng. Bull. 18(3) 1995). Volcano's successor: rules and tasks as objects, a memo of groups, interleaved exploration and implementation. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/graefe1995cascades "Summary") · [Paper](http://sites.computer.org/debull/95SEP-CD.pdf)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[EvoPrompt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.08532 "EvoPrompt: Connecting LLMs with Evolutionary Algorithms Yields Powerful Prompt Optimizers (2024)")**: Connecting LLMs with Evolutionary Algorithms Yields Powerful Prompt Optimizers (ICLR 2024). LLMs act as crossover and mutation operators (GA and DE) over a prompt population. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/guo2023evoprompt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.08532) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08532) · [Code](https://github.com/beeevita/EvoPrompt)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[SKILL-DISCO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.26669 "SKILL-DISCO: Distilling and Compiling Agent Traces into Reusable Procedural Skills (2026)")**: Distilling and Compiling Agent Traces into Reusable Procedural Skills (preprint 2026). Distills the procedure shared by an agent's successful traces and compiles it into callable, executable skills (abstract): Python procedures, not prompts (§3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/guo2026skilldisco "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.26669) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26669)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
