- **[SynSQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27261 "SynSQL: Synthesizing Relational Databases for Robust Evaluation of Text-to-SQL Systems (2026)")**: Synthesizing Relational Databases for Robust Evaluation of Text-to-SQL Systems (preprint, 2026). LLM-synthesized test databases conditioned on the NL question and schema, not the gold SQL. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/habibollah2026synsql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27261) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27261)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Program Better](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.14502 "Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Program Better (2022)")** (ICLR 2023 (the PDF's running header, p. 1), 2022). An LM writes programming puzzles and solutions, a Python interpreter keeps the correct ones, and the LM is fine-tuned on them (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/haluptzok2022teach "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.14502) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.14502)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[SPARQL 1.1 Query Language](#/papers/harris2013sparql "SPARQL 1.1 Query Language (2013)")** (W3C Recommendation 21 March 2013 (a standard, not a paper; editors Harris and Seaborne)). The W3C standard that "defines the syntax and semantics of the SPARQL query language for RDF" (abstract): graph pattern matching with optional parts, alternatives, negation, property paths, aggregates, subqueries and four query forms (§5–§16). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/harris2013sparql "Summary") · [Paper](http://www.w3.org/TR/2013/REC-sparql11-query-20130321/)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sparql">dsl-sparql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nondet-semantics">nondet-semantics</a></span>
- **[GEqO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.01280 "GEqO: ML-Accelerated Semantic Equivalence Detection (2023)")**: ML-Accelerated Semantic Equivalence Detection (PACMMOD 1(4) (SIGMOD 2024), 2023). Detects semantically equivalent SPJ subexpressions, aiming at cloud-scale workloads, for computation reuse; evaluated only on synthetic TPC-H/TPC-DS workloads (§7). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/haynes2024geqo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2401.01280) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01280) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3626710)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[VeriEQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.03193 "VeriEQL: Bounded Equivalence Verification for Complex SQL Queries with Integrity Constraints (2024)")**: Bounded Equivalence Verification for Complex SQL Queries with Integrity Constraints (OOPSLA 2024). Bounded equivalence verification of complex SQL with integrity constraints. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/he2024verieql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.03193) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03193) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3649849) · [Code: VeriEQL](https://github.com/VeriEQL/VeriEQL) · [Code: VeriEQL-artifact-evaluation](https://zenodo.org/records/10795614)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/bounded">bounded</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairs-check">pairs-check</a></span>
- **[Graphiti](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03182 "Graphiti: Bridging Graph and Relational Database Queries (2025)")**: Bridging Graph and Relational Database Queries (PACMPL 9(PLDI) 2025 (PLDI 2025)). Checks whether a Cypher (graph database) query and a SQL query are equivalent modulo a user-written database transformer, rules that say which relational database holds the same data as a given graph (abstract; §4, Defs. 4.3–4.6). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/he2025graphiti "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.03182) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03182) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3729319)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/bounded">bounded</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-graph">dsl-graph</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Verification Trap](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05170 "Verification Trap: Understanding Test-Time Selection Failures under False Premises in Code Generation (2026)")**: Understanding Test-Time Selection Failures under False Premises in Code Generation (EMNLP 2026). Inserts a false premise into coding task descriptions, with hidden tests unchanged, samples 64 candidate programs, and selects one by default with unit tests a verifier writes from the same prompt; a task is trapped when the pool held a hidden-test-correct program but the selector returned a wrong one (Trap@64) (abstract; §3.1; §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/he2026verificationtrap "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05170) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05170)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[LLMs Gaming Verifiers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15149 "LLMs Gaming Verifiers: RLVR can Lead to Reward Hacking (2026)")**: RLVR can Lead to Reward Hacking (LLM Reasoning Workshop @ ICLR 2026 (PDF header)). RLVR-trained models on inductive rule tasks enumerate instance labels instead of inducing a rule, which passes a verifier that checks only extensional correctness (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/helff2026gaming "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.15149) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15149) · [Code](https://github.com/ml-research/llms-gaming-verifiers)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Technical Report: Full-Stack Fine-Tuning for the Q Programming Language](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.06813 "Technical Report: Full-Stack Fine-Tuning for the Q Programming Language (2025)")** (preprint 2025 (Morgan Stanley and Prime Intellect)). A LeetCode-style benchmark for Q, the language of the kdb+ time-series database, which the authors call "much less present on the Internet" than mainstream languages (abstract; §2); frontier and base Qwen-2.5 models benchmarked on it (§4); Qwen-2.5 models from 1.5B to 32B adapted to Q by pretraining, supervised fine-tuning and RL (§5–7); models, code and data released (§1, §11). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/hogan2025qlang "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.06813) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06813) · [Code](https://github.com/morganstanley/MSML)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rarelang">rarelang</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[GRACE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09175 "Scoped Verification for Reliable Long-Horizon Agentic Context Evolution under Distribution Shift (2026)")**: Scoped Verification for Reliable Long-Horizon Agentic Context Evolution under Distribution Shift (preprint 2026). Keeps an agent's persistent system instruction as a typed semantic graph, updated from operational experience while the model, tools and harness stay fixed (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/hsu2026grace "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09175) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09175) · [Code](https://github.com/RedMind-Research/GRACE)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[ADAS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.08435 "Automated Design of Agentic Systems (2024)")**: Automated Design of Agentic Systems (ICLR 2025 (per the paper's running header, PDF p. 1), 2024). Meta Agent Search: a meta agent programs new agents in code (abstract), building on an archive of discovered agents (App. B). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/hu2024adas "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.08435) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08435) · [Code](https://github.com/ShengranHu/ADAS)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Qr-Hint](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.04352 "Qr-Hint: Actionable Hints Towards Correcting Wrong SQL Queries (2024)")**: Actionable Hints Towards Correcting Wrong SQL Queries (SIGMOD 2024 (PACMMOD)). Given a correct target query and a wrong working query, gives step-by-step repairs (repair sites and fixes), clause by clause from FROM to SELECT, that the authors claim provably lead to a query equivalent to the target (abstract; §1; Theorem 3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/hu2024qrhint "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.04352) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04352) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3654995) · [Code](https://github.com/yihaoh/qr-hint)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[AutoScraper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.12753 "AutoScraper: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation (2024)")**: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation (EMNLP 2024 (main)). Has an LLM write a web scraper for one website, a sequence of XPath expressions built from a few seed pages, which a parser then runs on the site's other pages without the LLM (abstract; §3.1; §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/huang2024autoscraper "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.12753) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12753) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.141) · [Code](https://github.com/EZ-hwh/AutoScraper)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-path">dsl-path</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[Self-Improvement in Language Models: The Sharpening Mechanism](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.01951 "Self-Improvement in Language Models: The Sharpening Mechanism (2025)")** (ICLR 2025). Formalizes self-improvement as "sharpening": the model acts as its own verifier (a self-reward such as its own likelihood) to shift probability onto high-quality responses, amortizing inference-time search into post-training (abstract; §1.1, §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/huang2024sharpening "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.01951) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01951)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/theory">theory</a></span>
- **[From Raw Experience to Skill Consumption: A Systematic Study of Model-Generated Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23899 "From Raw Experience to Skill Consumption: A Systematic Study of Model-Generated Agent Skills (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Measures whether one domain-level skill that an extractor model distills from a target model's own trajectories helps that target on held-out tasks, for five extractors, six targets and five agentic domains (ALFWorld, SpreadsheetBench, SWE-bench-Verified, SEAL-0, BFCL-v4 multi-turn) (abstract; §4.1, Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/huang2026skillconsumption "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23899) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23899) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/SkillLens)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[SPA](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08620 "SPA: A SQL-Plan-Aware Reinforcement Learning Framework for Query Rewriting with LLMs (2026)")**: A SQL-Plan-Aware Reinforcement Learning Framework for Query Rewriting with LLMs (preprint 2026). GRPO-trained rewriter with gated rewards: syntax, equivalence, plan change, measured latency. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/huang2026spa "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08620) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08620)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Selection Without Signal, Recovery Through Expression: A Measurement Study of Post-Hoc Falsification Operators for Frozen Small Code Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16999 "Selection Without Signal, Recovery Through Expression: A Measurement Study of Post-Hoc Falsification Operators for Frozen Small Code Models (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Measures whether post-hoc operators over a frozen small code model's own samples (selection, verification, repair, elimination, portfolios, sound vetoes, generation conditioning) beat Best-of-N, which deploys the first candidate that passes the visible tests; correctness is the hidden tests' verdict (abstract; §2). Models of at most 1.7B run locally (Qwen2.5-Coder 1.5B and 0.5B, DeepSeek-Coder 1.3B, Qwen3 probes) on a self-authored benchmark, an engineered one, HumanEval+ and MBPP+ (§3; Tab. 2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/iscan2026falsification "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16999) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16999)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[GRewriter](#/papers/jiang2025grewriter "GRewriter: Practical Query Rewriting with Automatic Rule Set Expansion in GaussDB (2025)")**: Practical Query Rewriting with Automatic Rule Set Expansion in GaussDB (PVLDB 18(12) 2025). Huawei GaussDB's rewriter: rules in G-DSL found by a WeTune-derived enumerator. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2025grewriter "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3750601.3750622)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairgen-apply">pairgen-apply</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[SQLGovernor (Tencent)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.08575 "SQLGovernor: An LLM-powered SQL Toolkit for Real World Application (2025)")**: SQLGovernor: An LLM-powered SQL Toolkit for Real World Application (preprint, 2025). Toolkit for SQL syntax correction, query rewriting, query modification and equivalence verification, with LLM-generated, expert-validated rules (abstract; §4.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2025sqlgovernor "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.08575) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08575) · [Code](https://github.com/TencentBigData-SiriusAI/SQLGovernor)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a></span>
- **[ACME](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3797134 "ACME: Automated Clause Mapping Engine for Testing Emerging Database Systems (2026)")**: Automated Clause Mapping Engine for Testing Emerging Database Systems (FSE 2026 (PACMSE)). An LLM maps emerging-DBMS clauses to equivalent relational SQL so the two engines can be tested differentially (abstract, §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2026acme "Summary") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3797134) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3797134) · [Code](https://github.com/YuanchengJiang/ACME)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a></span>
- **[Language Models as Higher-Order Planning Formalizers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.23844 "Language Models as Higher-Order Planning Formalizers (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Asks whether LLM formalizers, which translate a natural-language planning problem into PDDL for a classical planner, keep scaling when a short description denotes a very large planning instance ("unraveling problems"), and proposes the higher-order formalizer: the LLM writes a program (Python in the experiments) that, when run, emits the PDDL problem file, which then goes to the planner (abstract; §1; §3.1; §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2026higherorder "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.23844) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23844) · [Code](https://github.com/oj79/formalizer-scaling)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[RuleEvolve](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00650 "Self-Evolving Coding Rules for AI Coding Agents (2026)")**: Self-Evolving Coding Rules for AI Coding Agents (NeurIPS 2026). Evolves a pool of coding-agent rules: an LLM mutates candidates and a judge keeps the best (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jiang2026ruleevolve "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.00650) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00650)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Pervasive Annotation Errors Break Text-to-SQL Benchmarks and Leaderboards](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.08778 "Pervasive Annotation Errors Break Text-to-SQL Benchmarks and Leaderboards (2026)")** (PVLDB 19(5), 2026). Measures annotation error rates in BIRD Mini-Dev and Spider 2.0-Snow, with an LLM reviewer (SAR-Agent) whose flags SQL experts adjudicate, and re-ranks 16 open-source BIRD-leaderboard agents on a corrected BIRD Dev subset (abstract; §1, §4.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jin2026annotation "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.08778) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08778) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3796195.3796206) · [Code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/text_to_sql_benchmarks)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[SynTra (Synthetic Transfer)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.06827 "Teaching Language Models to Hallucinate Less with Synthetic Tasks (2023)")**: Teaching Language Models to Hallucinate Less with Synthetic Tasks (preprint 2023). Lowers hallucination on tasks that answer or summarize from a given context by optimizing on a synthetic task where a program checks every answer (names retrieval: list the first five names, from a list of 100 random names, whose first name starts with a given letter; a name not on the list is a hallucination), then reusing the result on realistic tasks (abstract; §2.2, §3.1.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/jones2023syntra "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.06827) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06827)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[LLM-Modulo ("LLMs Can't Plan](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.01817 "Position: LLMs Can't Plan, But Can Help Planning in LLM-Modulo Frameworks (2024)")**: Position: LLMs Can't Plan, But Can Help Planning in LLM-Modulo Frameworks (But Can Help Planning in LLM-Modulo Frameworks"), ICML 2024 position paper). Argues that autoregressive LLMs cannot plan or verify plans by themselves, so cannot improve by critiquing their own output, drawing mainly on the authors' earlier studies (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kambhampati2024llmmodulo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.01817) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01817)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/survey">survey</a></span>
- **[Harnessing Code Agents for Automatic Software Verification](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.06341 "Harnessing Code Agents for Automatic Software Verification (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Hands each Coq lemma to a general code agent (e.g. Claude Code) inside a verification harness, with no fixed proof strategy (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kan2026aria "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.06341) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06341) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CoqProver-Code-57BB)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a></span>

Claude Code optimizes a multi-step LLM pipeline inside a standard codebase. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kassianik2026fapo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.19605) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19605) · [Code](https://github.com/cisco-foundation-ai/fully-automated-prompt-optimization)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>

Order-sensitive SQL semantics mechanized in Rocq (FormalSQL); an LLM proof agent proves equivalence or proposes a counter-database. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ke2026logos "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15709) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15709) · [Code: FormalSQL](https://github.com/WindOctober/FormalSQL) · [Code: Logos](https://github.com/WindOctober/Logos)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nondet-semantics">nondet-semantics</a></span>

Declarative LM pipelines with a compiler that bootstraps and selects few-shot demonstrations (or finetunes) to maximize a metric; instructions are named as parameters, but this version focuses on demonstrations (§4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/khattab2023dspy "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.03714) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.03714) · [Code](https://github.com/stanfordnlp/dspy)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Beyond Rule-Based Mutation Testing: Test-Aware Mutant Generation Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35841 "Beyond Rule-Based Mutation Testing: Test-Aware Mutant Generation Using Large Language Models (2026)")** (ESEM 2026 (LIPIcs, per the PDF's title page)). Mutation testing with an LLM that sees the tests: given the problem statement, the reference solution and its few base tests in one prompt, the model must write a mutant (a wrong version of the program) that passes the base tests; the larger EvalPlus test suites then decide whether it is a real fault (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kiele2026mutants "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35841) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35841) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ESEM.2026.49) · [Code](https://github.com/codingWhale13/test-aware-mutants)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[data-aware NL2SQL candidate selection](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12319 "Data-aware candidate selection in NL2SQL translation via small separating instances (2026)")**: Data-aware candidate selection in NL2SQL translation via small separating instances (preprint 2026). Picks among text-to-SQL candidates using small separating database instances. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kikot2026separating "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12319) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12319) · [Code](https://github.com/staskikotx/SISelection)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/reduce">reduce</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[SkillSandbox](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.10088 "SkillSandbox: Skill Verification via Dynamic Scenario Synthesis (2026)")**: Skill Verification via Dynamic Scenario Synthesis (preprint 2026 (PDF p. 1 "Preprint")). Decides whether a skill an agent distilled from one task (a "condition" for when it applies and a "principle" to follow) enters the agent's library, by synthesizing new tasks and environments in which that condition arises and comparing runs with and without the skill (abstract; §3.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kim2026skillsandbox "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.10088) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10088) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/skillsandbox-647C/)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[When to Trust the Cheap Check](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.17633 "When to Trust the Cheap Check: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning (2026)")**: Weak and Strong Verification for Reasoning ((weak and strong verification, SSV), preprint 2026). A policy decides for each output whether to accept or reject it on a cheap "weak" verifier's score (self-consistency, proxy rewards) or to defer to a costly "strong" verifier (a user, a check against ground truth); its errors are incorrect acceptance (type I) and incorrect rejection (type II) relative to the strong verdict, plus how often the strong verifier is called (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kiyani2026weakstrong "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.17633) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17633) · [Code](https://github.com/nooranisima/weak-and-strong-verification)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[SpotIt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26840 "SpotIt: Evaluating Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Formal Verification (2026)")**: Evaluating Text-to-SQL Evaluation with Formal Verification (ICLR 2026). Evaluates text-to-SQL by searching, with bounded verification, for a database that separates generated and gold SQL. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/klopfenstein2025spotit "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26840) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26840) · [Code](https://github.com/ai-ar-research/SpotIt-plus)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/bounded">bounded</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[Can LLMs Normalize Databases? A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Schema Normalization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.11141 "Can LLMs Normalize Databases? A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Schema Normalization (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A benchmark of LLM database normalization from 1NF to BCNF; it reports recurring failures in dependency inference, schema decomposition and inter-table constraint reconstruction (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/koh2026dnbench "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.11141) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11141)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[RTRL (Round-Trip Reinforcement Learning)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01527 "Round-trip Reinforcement Learning: Self-Consistent Training for Better Chemical LLMs (2026)")**: Round-trip Reinforcement Learning: Self-Consistent Training for Better Chemical LLMs (COLM 2026). Trains a chemical LLM for round-trip consistency on paired tasks (molecule ↔ caption, reactants ↔ product): the model's output for an input is rewarded by how likely a frozen copy of the model finds the original input when mapping back, so the training data can be unlabelled inputs from one side only (abstract; §4, §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/kong2025rtrl "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.01527) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01527) · [Code](https://github.com/LechengKong/RTRL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>

- **[AlphaCode](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.07814 "Competition-Level Code Generation with AlphaCode (2022)")**: Competition-Level Code Generation with AlphaCode (Science 2022). Competitive programming: an encoder-decoder transformer, pre-trained on GitHub code and fine-tuned on the authors' CodeContests dataset, samples up to millions of C++ and Python programs per problem; programs that fail the problem statement's example tests are dropped, and the rest are clustered by their outputs on inputs written by a separate test-input model, choosing at most 10 submissions (abstract; §4.4–4.6). Evaluated on CodeContests and in simulated Codeforces contests (§5.1–5.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2022alphacode "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.07814) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.07814) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.abq1158) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/code_contests)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>

A first training stage before instruction tuning in which a model predicts, in a natural-language chain of thought, a function's output for a given input or a feasible input for a given output; the authors report gains over a single-stage baseline and over other first-stage datasets across symbolic, scientific, logic, math and commonsense benchmarks on four base models of 7B–27B parameters (abstract; §3.2, Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2025codeio "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.07316) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07316) · [Code](https://github.com/hkust-nlp/CodeIO)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>

- **[AdverMCTS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.10449 "AdverMCTS: Combating Pseudo-Correctness in Code Generation via Adversarial Monte Carlo Tree Search (2026)")**: Combating Pseudo-Correctness in Code Generation via Adversarial Monte Carlo Tree Search (preprint 2026). Code generation as a game between two MCTS agents on one LLM: a Solver searches over reasoning steps and writes programs, and an Attacker searches for test inputs on which the programs that pass the public tests disagree; an LLM arbiter labels each such input's expected output, and the labelled tests then screen new programs, penalize failing branches and re-rank the final candidates (abstract; §3.3–3.6). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2026advermcts "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.10449) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10449)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>

Runs ACE/GEPA-style prompt learning in parallel over many agent traces. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2026combee "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.04247) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04247)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

Small-model rewriter trained with SQL-GRPO on SQL-MCTS, a corpus of complex slow queries. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2026laser "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.06804) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06804)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Learning from Execution: Self-Evolving Memory for Private-Library Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.24222 "Learning from Execution: Self-Evolving Memory for Private-Library Code Generation (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Code generation against recently released Python libraries that stand in for an enterprise's private libraries, chosen to reduce "the chance that models have memorized their latest APIs" (§V-B); the authors report that even with every API document a task needs in the prompt, models still misuse single APIs, chain APIs wrongly and pick wrong solution strategies (§III, Tab. I, Fig. 1), and add to a RAG pipeline a memory of usage guidelines learned from running the generated code, with no weight updates (abstract; §IV). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2026privatelibrary "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.24222) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24222) · [Code](https://github.com/THU-Agent/MEMCoder)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rarelang">rarelang</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[ProgAgent: Learning Tool Orchestration through Programmatic Training](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14774 "ProgAgent: Learning Tool Orchestration through Programmatic Training (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Trains Qwen3-8B and 14B to call tools by writing Python programs that run in a persistent sandbox, keeping tool results in variables and printing only what they choose (programmatic tool calling, PTC), and compares this under one recipe with direct tool calling (DTC), where every tool result enters the context, and with a Hybrid of the two (abstract; §2.2; §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2026progagent "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14774) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14774) · [Code](https://github.com/hkust-nlp/ProgAgent)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/gym">gym</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[Self-Modifying Lean Proof Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17352 "Self-Modifying Lean Proof Agents with Verifier-Grounded Benchmark Coevolution (2026)")**: Self-Modifying Lean Proof Agents with Verifier-Grounded Benchmark Coevolution (preprint 2026). A fixed, trusted runtime wraps a mutable workspace (proof workflow, prompts, tools) that the agent rewrites between generations, around one fixed LLM backend (abstract; App. A). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/li2026selfmodifying "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17352) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17352)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[ADMITOR ("Admission Without Answers")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15565 "Admission Without Answers: Label-Free Certification and Experience Learning for LLM-Based Optimization Modeling (2026)")**: Admission Without Answers: Label-Free Certification and Experience Learning for LLM-Based Optimization Modeling (preprint 2026). A label-free admission gate for an agent that learns skills for optimization modeling (LLM-written LP and MILP models): three model families each write a model, every model is solved on the stated instance and on instances with resampled parameters, the largest group whose optimal values agree on every instance across families gives the accepted value, and a threshold fitted on solver-verified synthetic problems turns the agreement into accept, abstain or escalate, with a Clopper–Pearson bound on false certificates (abstract; §3, Prop. 2). Only the host's own trajectories whose answer equals the accepted value enter its skill library (§3, Step 5). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/lian2026admitor "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.15565) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15565) · [Code](https://github.com/junbolian/AdmitOR)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

- **[Can Large Language Models Generalize Procedures Across Representations?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.03542 "Can Large Language Models Generalize Procedures Across Representations? (2026)")** (ICML 2026 (PMLR 306)). Trains models on one of three isomorphic forms of the same planning task (an English how-to task with step durations and order constraints, a graph's adjacency list, a Python longest-path function to trace) and tests on all three; the authors report that "naive training on a single representation fails to reliably generalize across representations" across Qwen, Llama and Olmo models and four post-training methods (§3.2, Fig. 2; App. A.2), and propose a two-stage RL curriculum, symbolic data first (graph in the planning runs, code in math and physics), then English (abstract, §4, §4.4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/lin2026procedures "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.03542) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03542) · [Code](https://github.com/fangru-lin/procedure_generalization_llm)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>

Prolonged RL training with KL control, reference-policy resets and a diverse task suite (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/liu2025prorl "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.24864) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24864) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/085713-0608)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[BudgetAPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05671 "How Should a Prompt Optimizer Spend a Tight Budget? BudgetAPO with Noise-Adaptive Evaluation (2026)")**: How Should a Prompt Optimizer Spend a Tight Budget? BudgetAPO with Noise-Adaptive Evaluation (preprint 2026). A single-stage prompt optimizer for a tight budget of subject-model calls: a 16-call probe measures the task's per-row score spread, a closed-form rule sizes a fixed evaluation slice to a target standard error, every candidate is scored on that same slice so each accept/reject is a paired comparison, and an optimizer LLM rewrites the prompt's reasoning strategy and output format from up to three failing rows (abstract; §3.1–3.3). The authors call it the first APO method to size its evaluation to each task's measured noise before search (§1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026budgetapo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05671) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05671)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Counterexample Guided Learning in the Large using Reasoning Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11521 "Counterexample Guided Learning in the Large using Reasoning Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). An LLM learner infers a regular expression from labeled strings; a teacher builds automata for the candidate and the target, decides whether they are equivalent and, if not, returns strings on which they disagree as positive or negative counterexamples, and the loop repeats until equivalence or a round budget (abstract; §3; §4 "Teacher-Learner Iterative Framework"). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026cegl "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.11521) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11521) · [Code](https://github.com/Lhtie/CEGML)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-regex">dsl-regex</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[ESPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04197 "ESPO: Error-Structured Prompt Optimization via Diagnose, Diversify, and Stabilize (2026)")**: Error-Structured Prompt Optimization via Diagnose, Diversify, and Stabilize (EMNLP 2026). Error-structured prompt optimization: diagnose all errors at once, propose with four strategies, select by bootstrap stability. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026espo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04197) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04197)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[LLMs as a Jury](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10139 "LLMs as a Jury: Cross-Model Consensus Can Outperform Process Reward Models for LLM Reasoning (2026)")**: Cross-Model Consensus Can Outperform Process Reward Models for LLM Reasoning (preprint 2026). Agreement across independently trained models, each solving once, picks the answer from a Best-of-N pool; the authors report it beats self-consistency and self-scoring on seven benchmarks (Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026jury "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.10139) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10139)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[MIRA (Memory-Item Reuse and Adaptation)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.06950 "MIRA: Evidence-Verified Repair Memory for Text-to-SQL Correction (2026)")**: MIRA: Evidence-Verified Repair Memory for Text-to-SQL Correction (preprint 2026). A corrector for SQL from a text-to-SQL agent treated as a black box (§2.1) that reuses past confirmed corrections on the same database without weight updates: it splits each past correction into separate repair memory items, retrieves items for a new query, activates only those the question, the SQL and database observations support, and adapts them to the current SQL (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/liu2026mira "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.06950) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06950)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

Edits typed units of a structured prompt and links each edit to the examples it fixes or breaks. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ma2026sepo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.28067) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28067)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[SWE-Proof: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World Issues with Machine-Checked Proofs?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21190 "SWE-Proof: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World Issues with Machine-Checked Proofs? (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). A benchmark that gives each of the 500 SWE-bench Verified issues a formal specification, a verifying reference implementation and its proof under three backends (Nagini for Python, Velvet in Lean, and pure Lean with agent-written proofs), extended to the Python instances of SWE-bench Pro (abstract; §1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ma2026sweproof "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21190) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21190)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[Same Pattern](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.23032 "Same Pattern, Different Answer: A Reference Semantics and Divergence Map for GQL and SQL/PGQ Path Patterns (2026)")**: Same Pattern, Different Answer: A Reference Semantics and Divergence Map for GQL and SQL/PGQ Path Patterns (Different Answer (GQL and SQL/PGQ path patterns), preprint 2026 (a PVLDB-format submission copy is in the artifact repository)). Measures what graph engines return for path patterns whose meaning the two ISO standards GQL and SQL/PGQ fix in their shared core, GPML: a 17-construct suite on six releases of five products (Kùzu, DuckPGQ, two Neo4j releases, Memgraph, Apache AGE), each cell scored as conforming, diverging, rejected or inexpressible (abstract; §4–5). The authors develop one of the engines they measure (Samyama-Graph) and keep its row out of every statistic (§8). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/mandarapu2026divergence "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.23032) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23032) · [Code](https://github.com/samyama-ai/gpml-conformance)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dialect">dialect</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-graph">dsl-graph</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nondet-eval">nondet-eval</a></span>

LLMs discover DBMS test oracles as Constrained Abstract Queries (equivalent-query skeletons). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/mang2025oracles "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.06663) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06663) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3802017) · [Code](https://github.com/joyemang33/Argus)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nondet-eval">nondet-eval</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairgen-check">pairgen-check</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[Solving a Million-Step LLM Task with Zero Errors](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09030 "Solving a Million-Step LLM Task with Zero Errors (2025)")** (preprint 2025). Solves a Towers of Hanoi instance whose solution takes over one million LLM steps, with zero errors (abstract; §4.4); the solution strategy is given in every agent's prompt, so only execution is tested (§4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/meyerson2025maker "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09030) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09030) · [Code](https://github.com/cognizant-ai-lab/neuro-san-benchmarking)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[RATest](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.04467 "Explaining Wrong Queries Using Small Examples (2019)")**: Explaining Wrong Queries Using Small Examples (SIGMOD 2019). Given a separating database, looks for the smallest sub-instance that still separates a wrong query from the right one (abstract); its faster algorithm, used for the rest of the experiments (§7.1), minimizes the witness of one differing tuple (§4.2, Alg. 2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/miao2019ratest "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.04467) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.04467) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3299869.3319866)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/reduce">reduce</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[Program Semantic Inequivalence Game with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03818 "Program Semantic Inequivalence Game with Large Language Models (2025)")** (NeSy 2026 (PMLR vol. 284, per the v3 PDF's first-page header), 2025). A generator makes semantically different variants of real programs and an evaluator must find inputs on which they differ; the two train each other (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/micelibarone2025sinq "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03818) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03818) · [Code](https://github.com/Avmb/semantic_neq_game)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[Verifying SQL Queries using Theories of Tables and Relations](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.03057 "Verifying SQL Queries using Theories of Tables and Relations (2024)")** (LPAR 2024). Extends cvc5's SMT theories of bags (as a theory of tables) and of relations, and adds a theory of nullable sorts, for SQL queries with join, projection and selection (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/mohamed2024cvc5sql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.03057) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03057) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.29007/rlt7) · [Code](https://github.com/cvc5/cvc5)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[CypherLens: An Interactive Demo for Evaluating and Diagnosing NL-to-Cypher Systems](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol19/p4558-mohammadi.pdf "CypherLens: An Interactive Demo for Evaluating and Diagnosing NL-to-Cypher Systems (2026)")** (PVLDB 19(12) 2026, demo paper). A platform for evaluating NL-to-Cypher systems (English questions to Cypher, a graph-database query language) beyond execution accuracy: it runs generated and gold queries on Neo4j, aligns their result columns, scores Exact Match, Soft Match and Dice, and has LLM agents label query types and diagnose errors against a Cypher-specific error taxonomy (abstract; §2, PDF pp. 2–3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/mohammadi2026cypherlens "Summary") · [PDF](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol19/p4558-mohammadi.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3827998.3828065) · [Code](https://github.com/YasmeanMohammadi/cypherLenz)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-graph">dsl-graph</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[GRASP: Gated Regression-Aware Skill Proposer for Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.29668 "GRASP: Gated Regression-Aware Skill Proposer for Self-Improving LLM Agents (2026)")** (EMNLP 2026). Improves an LLM agent through edits to a bounded library of skills (Markdown instructions injected into its context), with no weight updates: an LLM labels each failed trace with a failure mode, and a skill writer proposes add, modify or remove edits for the most frequent modes (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/moll2026grasp "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.29668) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29668) · [Code](https://github.com/jomoll/GRASP)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Verifier Errors in RLVR](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35677 "Verifier Errors in RLVR: Reward Hacking, Limits of Feedback, and Selective Control (2026)")**: Reward Hacking, Limits of Feedback, and Selective Control (preprint 2026). When does reward rise while correctness falls under an imperfect verifier? A codebook of reusable instruction units; an encoder routes each input to a few, a generator composes the prompt. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/nath2026codebooks "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.28360) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28360)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Unnesting Arbitrary Queries](#/papers/neumann2015unnesting "Unnesting Arbitrary Queries (2015)")** (BTW 2015). Claims to unnest arbitrary correlated subqueries ("the dependent join can be eliminated from any query", §3.2) by pushing dependent joins down until they become regular joins. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/neumann2015unnesting "Summary") · [Paper](https://dl.gi.de/handle/20.500.12116/2418)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[Adaptive Text2GQL](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.804.pdf, "Adaptive Text2GQL: Integrating Structural Twig Linking and Evolutionary In-Context Learning (2026)")**: Integrating Structural Twig Linking and Evolutionary In-Context Learning (ACL 2026 (Volume 1: Long Papers; first-page footer, PDF p. 1)). A training-free pipeline that writes graph-database queries in Cypher, Gremlin and SPARQL from English questions: schema-filled query templates ("twigs") retrieved as hints, a few-shot example store that starts empty and grows during inference, and a correction agent that checks a query against regex rules and then runs it on the database, retrying with the error (abstract; §3–4, PDF pp. 1, 3–6). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/niu2026text2gql "Summary") · [PDF](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.804.pdf,) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.acl-long.804)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-graph">dsl-graph</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sparql">dsl-sparql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[AlphaEvolve](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.13131 "AlphaEvolve: A coding agent for scientific and algorithmic discovery (2025)")**: A coding agent for scientific and algorithmic discovery (preprint 2025 (Google DeepMind white paper)). An evolutionary coding agent: LLMs propose program changes, automated evaluators score them. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/novikov2025alphaevolve "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.13131) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13131) · [Code](https://github.com/google-deepmind/alphaevolve_results)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[QueryHouse](https://hacking.kaist.ac.kr/pubs/2026/oh:queryhouse.pdf "QueryHouse: Cross-DBMS Differential Testing with LLM and Query Transpilation (2026)")**: Cross-DBMS Differential Testing with LLM and Query Transpilation (ESORICS 2026 (Springer LNCS, pp. 701–721)). Cross-DBMS differential testing for logic bugs: each generated test case is transpiled into the dialects of Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, SQLite and MariaDB, run on all of them, and any difference goes to triage, as a bug if it contradicts official documentation, else as an undocumented inconsistency (§1, PDF p. 2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/oh2026queryhouse "Summary") · [PDF](https://hacking.kaist.ac.kr/pubs/2026/oh:queryhouse.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-032-38692-2_35) · [Code: QueryHouse](https://github.com/queryhouse-2037/QueryHouse) · [Code: QueryHouse-sqlglot](https://github.com/queryhouse-2037/QueryHouse-sqlglot)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/workload">workload</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[MIPRO (shipped in DSPy as MIPROv2)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.11695 "Optimizing Instructions and Demonstrations for Multi-Stage Language Model Programs (2024)")**: Optimizing Instructions and Demonstrations for Multi-Stage Language Model Programs (EMNLP 2024). Optimizes instructions and few-shot demonstrations of every module in a multi-stage LM program. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/opsahlong2024mipro "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.11695) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11695) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.525)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>
- **[Optimize Cheap](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10694 "Optimize Cheap, Deploy Strong: Cost-Aware Cross-Tier Transfer for Evolutionary Optimization (2026)")**: Optimize Cheap, Deploy Strong: Cost-Aware Cross-Tier Transfer for Evolutionary Optimization (Deploy Strong, preprint 2026). Runs evolutionary prompt search with a cheap answering model, a strong reflector, and deploys on a stronger model. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/oved2026crosstier "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10694) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10694)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Overfitting in Synthesis: Theory and Practice](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1905.07457 "Overfitting in Synthesis: Theory and Practice (2019)")** (CAV 2019). Observes that syntax-guided synthesis tools built on counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS) fail on problems they solve with a less expressive grammar once the grammar grows, and attributes part of it to overfitting: candidates that fit the examples so far but violate the specification (abstract, §2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/padhi2019overfitting "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1905.07457) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.07457) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-25540-4_17) · [Code](https://github.com/SaswatPadhi/LoopInvGen)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Logic-LM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.12295 "Logic-LM: Empowering Large Language Models with Symbolic Solvers for Faithful Logical Reasoning (2023)")**: Empowering Large Language Models with Symbolic Solvers for Faithful Logical Reasoning (Findings of EMNLP 2023). An LLM translates a logic problem stated in natural language into a symbolic formulation, and a deterministic solver infers the answer from it; when the solver returns an error, the LLM is prompted with the error message to revise the formulation (abstract; §3, §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/pan2023logiclm "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.12295) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.12295) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.findings-emnlp.248) · [Code](https://github.com/teacherpeterpan/Logic-LLM)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a></span>

An intent-centered NL2SQL metric meant to replace execution accuracy; an execution match still decides which LLM stage judges the prediction (§4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/pei2026rose "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.12988) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12988) · [Code](https://github.com/CedricPei/ROSE)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>

Evaluates text-to-SQL across SQL dialects by running each side on its own engine and comparing normalized results. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/perlitz2026polysql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.07796) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07796) · [Code](https://github.com/IBM/polysql)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>

An extensible rule engine that rewrites queries in the Query Graph Model (QGM) before plan optimization. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/pirahesh1992starburst "Summary") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/130283.130294) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/130283.130294)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/qo">qo</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-lib">rules-lib</a></span>
- **[Semantic Equivalence Self-Play (SEQ/SINQ](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17010 "Improving LLM Code Reasoning via Semantic Equivalence Self-Play with Formal Verification (2026)")**: Improving LLM Code Reasoning via Semantic Equivalence Self-Play with Formal Verification (Haskell), Findings of ACL 2026). A generator (Alice) writes a variant of a reference Haskell program, either equivalent with a Liquid Haskell proof (SEQ) or inequivalent with a diverging input that is executed (SINQ); an evaluator (Bob) judges whether the pair is equivalent, and its sampled success rate sets each instance's difficulty (abstract; §3.1, §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/poon2026selfplay "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17010) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17010) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.findings-acl.1615) · [Code](https://github.com/Baki-0501/llm-self-play-liquidhaskell)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>
- **[SQLRepair](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2102.05729 "SQLRepair: Identifying and Repairing Mistakes in Student-Authored SQL Queries (2021)")**: Identifying and Repairing Mistakes in Student-Authored SQL Queries (ICSE-SEET 2021). Collects the SQL queries that undergraduates wrote for programming-by-example problems, each given as (source, destination) table pairs that a query must turn one into the other, and classifies their syntax and semantic errors (abstract; §II; §IV-A). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/preslermarshall2021sqlrepair "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2102.05729) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.05729) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICSE-SEET52601.2021.00030) · [Code](https://github.com/kpresler/SQLRepair)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[ProTeGi](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.03495 "Automatic Prompt Optimization with 'Gradient Descent' and Beam Search (2023)")**: Automatic Prompt Optimization with "Gradient Descent" and Beam Search (EMNLP 2023). Natural-language "gradients": an LLM criticizes a prompt on errors and edits it. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/pryzant2023protegi "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.03495) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.03495) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.494) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/LMOps)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Auditing Reward Hackability in Code RL Training Environments](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16062 "Auditing Reward Hackability in Code RL Training Environments (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Measures how often a code task's test suite accepts a wrong solution: Claude Sonnet 4 writes incorrect patches meant to pass the existing tests, in up to three rounds that read the failure logs, and each is run with the project's own test runner in the task's Docker container; a sample of SWE-bench Verified tasks from two repositories (astropy, django) and of R2E-Gym tasks (abstract; §3.1; §4; §7). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/rajan2026hackability "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.16062) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16062)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Resource-Aware Neuro-Symbolic Reasoning for Local Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27281 "Resource-Aware Neuro-Symbolic Reasoning for Local Small Language Models (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A small model run locally translates an ordering problem, in one call, into a typed finite-domain program of entities, safe Horn-style rules and pairwise or fixed-position constraints, each with the source text it comes from; a check that every constraint is grounded in that text, deterministic local repairs, and an exact solver then produce the answer, in place of repeated sampling (abstract; §5; §3 "Operational semantics of the typed fragment"). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ramirez2026vfr "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.27281) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27281)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a></span>
- **[Correction and Corruption](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.18245 "Correction and Corruption: A Two-Rate View of Error Flow in LLM Protocols (2026)")**: A Two-Rate View of Error Flow in LLM Protocols (preprint 2026). A paired audit of an added model call: success before and after on the same instances, under one binary rule (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/reitich2026correction "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.18245) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18245)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
