Lean 4 provers (671B, abstract; 7B, §2.3) trained with RL after a cold start (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ren2025deepseekproverv2 "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.21801) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21801) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Prover-V2)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[A Formalization of SQL with Nulls](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2003.11331 "A Formalization of SQL with Nulls (2022)")** (J. Autom. Reasoning 66(4) 2022). A Coq mechanization of SQL semantics with set and bag operations, lateral joins, nested subqueries and NULLs, with key metatheoretic properties validated (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ricciotti2020nulls "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2003.11331) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.11331) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10817-022-09632-4) · [Code](https://github.com/wricciot/nullSQL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[ContraPrompt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17937 "ContraPrompt: Contrastive Prompt Optimization via Dyadic Reasoning Trace Analysis (2026)")**: Contrastive Prompt Optimization via Dyadic Reasoning Trace Analysis (ICLR 2026 LLM Reasoning Workshop (PDF running head)). Contrastive optimization from paired reasoning traces (a failed and a successful attempt on the same input; abstract, §1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/rishav2026contraprompt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.17937) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17937) · [Code](https://github.com/rishvv/contraprompt_artefacts)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[λ-RLM ("The Y-Combinator for LLMs")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.20105 "The Y-Combinator for LLMs: Solving Long-Context Rot with $\lambda$-Calculus (2026)")**: The Y-Combinator for LLMs: Solving Long-Context Rot with $\lambda$-Calculus (preprint 2026). Replaces the open-ended loop of Recursive Language Models, in which the model writes Python in a REPL over a prompt stored as a variable, with a fixed library of typed combinators (Split, Peek, Map, Filter, Reduce, Concat, Cross) run by a deterministic executor; the LLM picks a task type from a menu once and is otherwise called on leaf chunks small enough for its context window, plus a final synthesis call for some task types (abstract; §3.1, Tab. 1; §3.2, Alg. 1–2; App. D). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/roy2026ycombinator "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.20105) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20105) · [Code](https://github.com/lambda-calculus-LLM/lambda-RLM)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a></span>
- **[Weaver](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18203 "Shrinking the Generation-Verification Gap with Weak Verifiers (2025)")**: Shrinking the Generation-Verification Gap with Weak Verifiers (NeurIPS 2025). Builds a strong verifier from many weak ones (LM judges, reward models) to rank sampled answers (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/saadfalcon2025weaver "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18203) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18203)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Self-Generated In-Context Examples Improve LLM Agents for Sequential Decision-Making Tasks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00234 "Self-Generated In-Context Examples Improve LLM Agents for Sequential Decision-Making Tasks (2025)")** (preprint 2025). A ReAct-style agent keeps every trajectory that succeeds on a training task in a database and retrieves its nearest stored trajectories as in-context examples at each plan, reasoning and action step, starting from a few human-written ones (§2.2, §5.1); no weights change (App. C fine-tunes only as a side test). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sarukkai2025selfgenerated "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00234) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00234)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[RLMOpt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10471 "RLMOpt: Adaptive Prompt Optimization via Recursive Language Models (2026)")**: Adaptive Prompt Optimization via Recursive Language Models (preprint 2026). A recursive language model runs the search policy itself: inspects failures, allocates budget, decides when to stop. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/satheesha2026rlmopt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.10471) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10471)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[SAMMO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.02319 "Symbolic Prompt Program Search: A Structure-Aware Approach to Efficient Compile-Time Prompt Optimization (2024)")**: Symbolic Prompt Program Search: A Structure-Aware Approach to Efficient Compile-Time Prompt Optimization (Findings of EMNLP 2024). Compile-time search over prompt programs represented symbolically (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/schnabel2024sammo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2404.02319) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02319) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-emnlp.37) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/sammo)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[VaG ("When Self-Evolution Backfires")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05810 "When Self-Evolution Backfires: Pre-Commit Gating against Skill Contamination in LLM Agents (2026)")**: When Self-Evolution Backfires: Pre-Commit Gating against Skill Contamination in LLM Agents (preprint 2026). Finds that a self-evolving agent that admits every skill it distills from its own trajectories improves, peaks and then degrades as the pool grows, and argues that removing a defective skill afterwards cannot undo the skills later distilled with it in context (abstract; Introduction; Methodology). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/shang2026vag "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05810) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05810)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[DeepSeekMath](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03300 "DeepSeekMath: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models (2024)")**: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models (preprint 2024). Math pretraining on web data, then RL. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/shao2024deepseekmath "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03300) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03300) · [Code](https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-Math)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08106 "PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Treats the keep-it-if-the-dev-score-went-up step of self-evolving agents as uncontrolled adaptive multiple testing on a reused dev set, and replaces it with a commit gate: candidate and incumbent are scored on the same items, and a testing-by-betting e-process on the discordant pairs commits once its wealth reaches 1/α (abstract; §3; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/shawn2026pace "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.08106) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08106)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a></span>
- **[CSC-SQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.13271 "CSC-SQL: Corrective Self-Consistency in Text-to-SQL via Reinforcement Learning (2025)")**: Corrective Self-Consistency in Text-to-SQL via Reinforcement Learning (Findings of IJCNLP-AACL 2025). Takes the two most frequent sampled SQL outputs and has a merge-revision model correct them. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sheng2025cscsql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.13271) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13271) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-ijcnlp.91) · [Code](https://github.com/CycloneBoy/csc_sql)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[SKILL-KD: Contrastive Skill Distillation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28048 "SKILL-KD: Contrastive Skill Distillation for LLM Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Improves a frozen weaker student agent through a textual skill library written from a stronger teacher's runs: when the student fails a training task, the teacher runs the same task and a consolidation agent turns the gap between the two trajectories into a rule for the student's prompt (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/shi2026skillkd "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.28048) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28048)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[AutoPrompt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.15980 "AutoPrompt: Eliciting Knowledge from Language Models with Automatically Generated Prompts (2020)")**: Eliciting Knowledge from Language Models with Automatically Generated Prompts (EMNLP 2020). Searches discrete trigger tokens that elicit knowledge from masked language models. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/shin2020autoprompt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.15980) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.15980) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2020.emnlp-main.346) · [Code](https://github.com/ucinlp/autoprompt)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>
- **[Reflexion](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.11366 "Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning (2023)")**: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning (preprint 2023). The agent writes a verbal reflection after a failed attempt and keeps it in memory for the next attempt at the same task. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/shinn2023reflexion "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.11366) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.11366) · [Code](https://github.com/noahshinn/reflexion)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a></span>
- **[From Context to Skills: Can Language Models Learn from Context Skillfully?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27660 "From Context to Skills: Can Language Models Learn from Context Skillfully? (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Builds a natural-language skill set for one long, unseen context (a short Markdown file prepended to the system prompt) so that an LLM answers new tasks over that context better, with no human annotation, no external feedback and no weight updates (abstract; §3.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/si2026ctx2skill "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.27660) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27660) · [Code](https://github.com/S1s-Z/Ctx2Skill)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Reasoning Core](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05148 "Reasoning Core: Designing Broad Procedural Data for Completion-Supervised Reasoning Training (2026)")**: Designing Broad Procedural Data for Completion-Supervised Reasoning Training (preprint 2026). A library of procedural generators of checkable reasoning tasks (e.g. mathematics, logic, planning, formal languages, structured data, code), each with a semantic scorer and difficulty controls, compared under matched supervised fine-tuning with Procedural Warmup, Reasoning Gym and SynLogic on held-out benchmarks (abstract; §3, Tab. 1; §4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sileo2026reasoningcore "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05148) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05148) · [Code](https://github.com/sileod/reasoning-core)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/gym">gym</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[Can the Rookies Cut the Tough Cookie? Exploring the Use of LLMs for SQL Equivalence Checking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.05561 "Can the Rookies Cut the Tough Cookie? Exploring the Use of LLMs for SQL Equivalence Checking (2024)")** (preprint, 2024). A harder, realistic equivalence benchmark; most LLMs are biased towards "equivalent", GPT-4 excepted (§5). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/singh2024sqlequiquest "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.05561) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05561) · [Code](https://github.com/rajatb115/LLMs-for-SQL-Equivalence-Checking)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairs-check">pairs-check</a></span>
- **[Coding Agents are Strong Prompt Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26261 "Coding Agents are Strong Prompt Optimizers (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A coding agent analyzes a static corpus of trajectories with its own code and writes behavioral rules into the prompt. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026casd "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26261) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26261)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[RuleFlow](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.09051 "RuleFlow: Generating Reusable Program Optimizations with LLMs (2026)")**: Generating Reusable Program Optimizations with LLMs (preprint 2026). Optimizes pandas notebooks (not SQL) by turning LLM-found rewrites of single code cells into general rewrite rules with runtime preconditions, which a deterministic compiler then applies to unseen notebooks with no LLM calls (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026ruleflow "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.09051) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09051) · [Code](https://github.com/ADAPT-uiuc/RuleFlow)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a></span>
- **[The Hallucination Snowball](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.14588 "The Hallucination Snowball: Modeling Error Propagation as State Transitions in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines (2026)")**: Modeling Error Propagation as State Transitions in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines (ICML 2026 FAGEN workshop). Injects wrong numbers into the first agent's output of a four-agent LangGraph pipeline (Researcher → Analyst → Writer → Reviewer) on FinanceBench questions and tracks at each stage whether a gpt-4o judge without ground truth and a numeric matcher against reference values still detect them (§3.1–3.2); a second experiment compares five LLMs as skeptics at the first stage (§3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/singh2026snowball "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.14588) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14588) · [Code](https://github.com/prabhjotschugh/hallucination-snowball)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Massive Stochastic Testing of SQL](https://www.vldb.org/conf/1998/p618.pdf "Massive Stochastic Testing of SQL (1998)")** (VLDB 1998). Generates random SQL statements and runs each one on several vendors' DBMSs, comparing row counts and a checksum of the results (§2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/slutz1998rags "Summary") · [PDF](https://www.vldb.org/conf/1998/p618.pdf)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dialect">dialect</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/reduce">reduce</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/workload">workload</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[The Optimizer's Curse](https://jimsmith.host.dartmouth.edu/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/The_Optimizers_Curse.pdf "The Optimizer's Curse: Skepticism and Postdecision Surprise in Decision Analysis (2006)")**: Skepticism and Postdecision Surprise in Decision Analysis (Management Science 2006). Shows that choosing the alternative with the highest estimated value makes that estimate biased high even when every estimate is unbiased, so a decision maker should expect to be disappointed on average (abstract; §2.3, Prop. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/smith2006optimizerscurse "Summary") · [PDF](https://jimsmith.host.dartmouth.edu/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/The_Optimizers_Curse.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1287/mnsc.1050.0451)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/theory">theory</a></span>
- **[compute-optimal test-time scaling](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.03314 "Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally can be More Effective than Scaling Model Parameters (2024)")**: Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally can be More Effective than Scaling Model Parameters (preprint 2024). - **[QUITE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.07675 "QUITE: A Query Rewrite System Beyond Rules with LLM Agents (2025)")**: A Query Rewrite System Beyond Rules with LLM Agents (preprint, 2025). FSM-driven multi-agent LLM rewriter with database feedback. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/song2025quite "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.07675) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07675) · [Code](https://github.com/Yuyang-Song/QUITE)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[SketchOps](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.26448 "Mergeable Model-Side Aggregation States for Long-Context Language Models (2026)")**: Mergeable Model-Side Aggregation States for Long-Context Language Models (preprint 2026). Keeps fixed-size HyperLogLog sketch states beside a frozen LLM for set-based aggregation over long inputs (distinct counts, union size, Jaccard similarity, containment, grouped distinct counts; a Count–Min state for point frequencies) and appends their readouts to the model's input as evidence for its final decision, without "an additional generate–execute–return cycle" (abstract; §4; App. D). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/song2026sketchops "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.26448) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26448) · [Code](https://github.com/songdc98/sketchops)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a></span>
- **[Deep Language Networks](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.12509 "Joint Prompt Optimization of Stacked LLMs using Variational Inference (2023)")**: Joint Prompt Optimization of Stacked LLMs using Variational Inference (NeurIPS 2023). Two stacked LLM calls treated as layers whose prompts are learned jointly. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sordoni2023dln "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.12509) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.12509) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/deep-language-networks)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Recursive self-improvement of AI research agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26457 "Recursive self-improvement of AI research agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (Weco AI)). An outer-loop research agent rewrites the code of an inner-loop tree-search agent (a pared-down AIDE), grades each rewrite on ML-engineering, heuristic-algorithm and harness-engineering tasks under a fixed dollar budget, and keeps the best-graded agent as the next one to edit (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/srikanth2026aide2 "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.26457) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26457)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Sparks of Tabular Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00016 "Sparks of Tabular Reasoning via Text2SQL Reinforcement Learning (2025)")**: Sparks of Tabular Reasoning via Text2SQL Reinforcement Learning (Table Representation Learning Workshop (ACL) 2025). Trains two 7–8B models on text-to-SQL only (§4.1), then tests whether the gain carries over to answering questions over tables (CRT-QA) with no SQL output required (§1, Fig. 1, §4.5). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/stoisser2025sparks "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.00016) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00016) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.trl-1.20) · [Code](https://github.com/josefastoisser/sparks_of_tabular_reasoning)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a></span>
- **[STRuCT-LLM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.21575 "STRuCT-LLM: Unifying Tabular and Graph Reasoning with Reinforcement Learning for Semantic Parsing (2025)")**: Unifying Tabular and Graph Reasoning with Reinforcement Learning for Semantic Parsing (preprint 2025). Trains Qwen models (1.5B to 32B) on text-to-SQL and text-to-Cypher together, then measures transfer: SQL-only, Cypher-only and joint runs compared on Spider, BIRD Mini-Dev, EHRSQL and Text2Cypher (§4.4, Tab. 1), zero-shot on table and knowledge-graph QA (CRT-QA, TableBench, CR-LT-KGQA; §4.5, Tab. 2) and on SM3, whose questions come with equivalent SQL, Cypher and MongoDB queries (App. C, Tab. C1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/stoisser2025structllm "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.21575) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21575) · [Code](https://github.com/bouv/STRuCT-LLM)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-graph">dsl-graph</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a></span>
- **[Easy-to-Hard Generalization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.09472 "Easy-to-Hard Generalization: Scalable Alignment Beyond Human Supervision (2024)")**: Scalable Alignment Beyond Human Supervision (NeurIPS 2024). Reward models trained only on MATH levels 1–3 (on final-answer labels, step labels from PRM800K or Math-Shepherd, or both) score solutions to levels 4–5; the authors report that these evaluators generalize from easy to hard better than the generators trained on the same easy data (§4.2.1), and that an easy-trained outcome reward model also helps on hard APPS coding problems (§4.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sun2024easytohard "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.09472) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09472) · [Code](https://github.com/Edward-Sun/easy-to-hard)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>

Reliable Self-Improvement When Agents Reuse Their Benchmarks (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A certified evaluation and promotion rule for self-improvement loops that reuse one fixed evaluation set: with probability at least 1−α every promoted change is a genuine improvement on the task distribution (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sun2026reuse "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.33180) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33180)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a></span>

Reflective instruction optimization combined with few-shot reference examples. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sundararaman2026flare "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.02919) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02919) · [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/FLARE---Few-shot-Learning-based-Adaptive-Reflective-Engine)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[LRR (Localized Regex Repair)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.09037 "Repairing Regex Vulnerabilities via Localization-Guided Instructions (2026)")**: Repairing Regex Vulnerabilities via Localization-Guided Instructions (EACL 2026 (Volume 1: Long Papers)). Repairs regular expressions vulnerable to ReDoS (crafted inputs that make the regex engine's matching super-linear): a symbolic module localizes the vulnerable sub-pattern, then an LLM, prompted with that sub-pattern and five chain-of-thought repair examples, rewrites the whole regex (abstract; §4; App. B). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/sung2025lrr "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.09037) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09037) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-long.379) · [Code](https://github.com/cdltlehf/LRR)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rewrite">rewrite</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-regex">dsl-regex</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Grounding Agent Memory: Environment-Probing Curation for Enterprise Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.11060 "Grounding Agent Memory: Environment-Probing Curation for Enterprise Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). After each task a separate curator agent writes the agent's long-term memory from the trajectory and its grade (§3.2); this paper gives that curator read-only environment tools to check, narrow or refresh a candidate memory before committing it ("propose–probe–commit", §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/suresh2026grounding "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.11060) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11060)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>

S2CLite, a purely rule-based SPARQL-to-Cypher translator that needs neither the RDF graph nor external tools, compared with S2CTrans on BSBM42 and Spider4SPARQL; and Spider4SSC, a dataset pairing natural-language questions with SQL, SPARQL and Cypher queries over Spider's databases (abstract; §2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/vejvar2025spider4ssc "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.09354) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09354) · [Code](https://github.com/vejvarm/S2CLite)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/pairs">pairs</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-graph">dsl-graph</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sparql">dsl-sparql</a></span>

Verifies equivalence (and refinement) of two database-driven programs over different schemas, as in schema refactoring (abstract; refinement, §1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2017mediator "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.07660) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.07660) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3158144)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>

Few-shot generation of programs in domain-specific languages (DSLs): each demonstration carries the minimal subset of the DSL's BNF grammar needed for its output, and the LLM first predicts such a grammar for the test input, then the program (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2023grammar "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.19234) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19234) · [Code](https://github.com/berlino/grammar-prompting)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>

Proves Isabelle theorems with ChatGPT block by block: a decomposer proposes sub-goal lemmas, a formalizer writes the proof using lemmas retrieved from a growing skill library, and an evolver generalizes library lemmas and proves the requested sub-goals (abstract; §3; ChatGPT, §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2023legoprover "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.00656) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.00656) · [Code](https://github.com/wiio12/LEGO-Prover)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

A GPT-4 agent in Minecraft that writes its actions as code and keeps the programs that work in an ever-growing skill library, indexed by the embedding of their description and retrieved for new tasks; an automatic curriculum proposes the next task (abstract; §2.1–2.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2023voyager "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.16291) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16291) · [Code](https://github.com/MineDojo/Voyager)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>

Q-expressions (semiring semantics), with SMT used in normalization and unification; NULLs, keys and foreign keys modelled. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2024qed "Summary") · [PDF](https://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol17/p3602-wang.pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3681954.3682024)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[Measuring all the noises of LLM Evals](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21326 "Measuring all the noises of LLM Evals (2025)")** (preprint 2025 (v3)). Splits the noise of an LLM eval into prediction noise (different answers to the same question), data noise (which questions were sampled) and their total, by the law of total variance, and proposes "all-pairs comparison": paired comparison on every pair of models, measuring all three components (abstract; §1; §3.1; §4.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025evalnoise "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.21326) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21326) · [Code](https://github.com/all-the-noises/eval-arena)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>

Jointly searches an agent's graph (modules, flow) and each node's configuration. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025maestro "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.04642) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04642)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[QLCoder](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.08462 "QLCoder: A Query Synthesizer For Static Analysis of Security Vulnerabilities (2026)")**: A Query Synthesizer For Static Analysis of Security Vulnerabilities (ICLR 2026). An agent built on Claude Code that writes a CodeQL static-analysis query from a CVE's metadata; a query counts as successful only if it compiles, reports a dataflow path through the patched code in the vulnerable version, and reports none through it in the fixed version (§3.1); the authors report success for 53.4% of 176 Java CVEs (abstract; Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2025qlcoder "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.08462) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08462) · [Code](https://github.com/neuralprogram/qlcoder)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl2dsl">nl2dsl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-datalog">dsl-datalog</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Not All Skills Help: Measuring and Repairing Agent Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15390 "Not All Skills Help: Measuring and Repairing Agent Knowledge (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Curates an agent's natural-language skill library by measurement rather than LLM judgment: randomized masking on a small development set gives each skill a causal score per development task, from which it splits skills whose effect varies across tasks into conditional variants (an LLM rewrites them, behind a development-set gate), retires inert ones, merges near-duplicates, and at inference suppresses the skills predicted to hurt each test task, using its nearest development tasks (abstract; §2.1–2.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026assay "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.15390) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15390)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[Do Agent Optimizers Compound?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.14004 "Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0 (2026)")**: Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0 (technical report 2026 (RELAI)). A two-phase continual-learning test on hard Terminal-Bench 2.0 tasks: optimize, then optimize again after new tasks arrive (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026compound "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.14004) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14004) · [Code](https://github.com/relai-ai/Continual-Learning-Terminal-Bench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Unified Multi-Dimensional Benchmark for Complex Graph Reasoning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12391 "Unified Multi-Dimensional Benchmark for Complex Graph Reasoning in Large Language Models (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A semi-automatic framework in which an LLM writes composite graph tasks, their graph generators, reference solvers, graph-loading and evaluation scripts, with human-written tests for the solvers and an LLM quality filter, and a benchmark built with it that varies graph size (10 to 10,000 nodes), the number of composed seed tasks, explicit or story-like descriptions, graph in the prompt or in a file, and textbook or LeetCode-style task sources (abstract; §1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026graphgym "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12391) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12391)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/gym">gym</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[LLM-driven index tuning on SQL Server](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09181 "Evaluating the Practical Effectiveness of LLM-Driven Index Tuning on Microsoft SQL Server (2026)")**: Evaluating the Practical Effectiveness of LLM-Driven Index Tuning on Microsoft SQL Server (PVLDB 19(12) 2026). Compares LLM index recommendations with Microsoft's Database Tuning Advisor (DTA) on industrial benchmarks and real enterprise workloads (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026indextuning "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09181) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09181)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[LARA (Lookahead Automata ReDoS Analyzer)](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3832783.3837475 "LARA: Static ReDoS Detection for Regular Expressions with Lookahead via Matching Automata (2026)")**: LARA: Static ReDoS Detection for Regular Expressions with Lookahead via Matching Automata (ASE 2026). A static detector of ReDoS (regexes whose backtracking match time grows super-linearly with the input) for regexes with lookahead assertions: it compiles the regex into an automaton with lookahead transitions, replaces each lookahead by ε-transitions into the lookahead's own automaton (the "matching NFA"), and runs classical ambiguity analysis on the result (abstract, PDF p. 1; §3.1, PDF p. 4). The authors state the matching NFA is not language-equivalent to the regex, and prove that on every input its degree of match, a count of the (state, position) pairs backtracking visits, equals the regex's lookahead-aware count (Thm. 3.3, PDF p. 5; the proof is in supplementary material the PDF does not link). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026lara "Summary") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3832783.3837475) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3832783.3837475)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-regex">dsl-regex</a></span>
- **[SAGE (Statistical Acceptance Gate)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.36043 "SAGE: A Statistical Acceptance Gate for Self-Evolving Agents (2026)")**: SAGE: A Statistical Acceptance Gate for Self-Evolving Agents (preprint 2026). An acceptance gate for agents that evolve a skill document: instead of keeping any edit that raises the aggregate validation score, it compares the current and the edited skill item by item, weighs a regression more than a repair, caps the share of solved items an edit may break, and commits only when a one-sided exact binomial test on the discordant items rejects, abstaining otherwise (abstract; §3.2–3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026sage "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.36043) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36043)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Certified Long-Horizon Code Agent Evolution via Validation-Gated Skill Optimization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32990 "Certified Long-Horizon Code Agent Evolution via Validation-Gated Skill Optimization (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Long-horizon self-evolution of a code agent with frozen weights: after a task with mixed outcomes, the same LLM contrasts successful and failed trajectories to propose skills and principles, which join a persistent bank only if a paired rerun on a fixed 50-task validation panel gains at least 0.02 in pass rate; a retriever that scores embedding similarity, word overlap and an importance prior (App. B.5) picks items for later tasks (§1; §4.1; App. B.6). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wang2026valve "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.32990) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32990)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[CodeARC: Benchmarking Reasoning Capabilities of LLM Agents for Inductive Program Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.23145 "CodeARC: Benchmarking Reasoning Capabilities of LLM Agents for Inductive Program Synthesis (2025)")** (COLM 2025 (per the arXiv v2 PDF's running header)). A benchmark of inductive program synthesis (programming by example) for LLM agents: hidden Python functions taken from HumanEval+, MBPP+ and APPS, shown only through input-output examples, with their original names kept or replaced by a generic one (abstract; §3.3; §4, Tab. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wei2025codearc "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.23145) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23145) · [Code](https://github.com/Anjiang-Wei/CodeARC)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a></span>
- **[Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards Implicitly Incentivizes Correct Reasoning in Base LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14245 "Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards Implicitly Incentivizes Correct Reasoning in Base LLMs (2025)")** (preprint 2025). Revisits pass@k for math and code, and introduces CoT-Pass@K, which also requires correct reasoning steps, judged by an LLM (DeepSeek-R1-0528-Qwen3-8B, §1, §3.1) (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wen2025rlvr "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.14245) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14245)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>

Self-play where a formal verifier (Verus) checks generated programs, and a proposer writes new specifications at difficulty levels set from the solver's pass rates, easy ones included (§3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wilf2025psv "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.18160) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18160) · [Code](https://github.com/abwilf/psv)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>

- **[VRR-Stop ("Verify](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17641 "Verify, Repair, Repeat, or Stop? Robust Stopping for Noisy Verify-Repair Loops in LLM Agents (2026)")**: Verify, Repair, Repeat, or Stop? Robust Stopping for Noisy Verify-Repair Loops in LLM Agents (Repair, Repeat, or Stop?"), preprint 2026). When to stop an LLM's verify-repair loop when both the verifier and the repairer err: a four-parameter model (false accepts, false rejects, repairs, damage) turns each round's repeated verifier votes into a belief that the current plan is valid, and the loop repairs only while the expected gain of one more round is positive; VRR-Guard, a fallback for verifiers that barely separate valid from invalid plans, keeps the best candidate and replaces it only on a margin of votes (abstract; §3.2; §4.1–4.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/wu2026vrrstop "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.17641) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17641) · [Code](https://anonymous.4open.science/r/vrr-artifact-2583)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Logic-RL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.14768 "Logic-RL: Unleashing LLM Reasoning with Rule-Based Reinforcement Learning (2025)")**: Unleashing LLM Reasoning with Rule-Based Reinforcement Learning (preprint 2025). RL with a rule-based reward on procedurally generated Knights and Knaves logic puzzles trains Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-1M; the authors report that it develops reasoning skills such as reflection, verification and summarization that are absent from the logic corpus, and generalizes to the math benchmarks AIME and AMC (abstract; §4 "RQ 4"). Seven research questions cover, among others, RL algorithms, SFT against RL, curricula and response length (§4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xie2025logicrl "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.14768) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14768) · [Code](https://github.com/Unakar/Logic-RL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>

Self-play that optimizes the inference-time context of agents in multi-turn, multi-agent text games (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xie2026memo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.09022) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09022) · [Code](https://github.com/openverse-ai/MEMO)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>

Evolves several populations of natural-language skills from experience and verifier feedback. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xiong2026dive "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12486) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12486)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>

An LLM reads a natural-language input, splits it into its problems and labels each with the kind of formal reasoning it needs (logic programming, first-order logic, constraint satisfaction or SMT); a router sends each problem to the matching solver, whose LLM front end translates it into that solver's language (abstract; §2, Fig. 1, Alg. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xu2025adaptive "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.06774) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06774) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-long.54) · [Code](https://github.com/idiap/adaptive_symbolic_reasoning)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a></span>

Budget-aware evolution of agent harnesses (instructions, tools, runtime components) (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026harnesslens "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.27311) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27311) · [Code](https://github.com/jhxu5214/HarnessLens)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Phantom Gains](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.20290 "Phantom Gains: Auditing Self-Improvement Against a Measured Null (2026)")**: Auditing Self-Improvement Against a Measured Null (preprint 2026 (under review, venue not named)). Audits per-problem claims about self-improvement (which problems a model learned, lost or newly reached) by passing a frozen, untrained model through the same pipeline as every trained arm, so that any transition the frozen model shows is a measurement artifact (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026phantomgains "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.20290) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20290) · [Code](https://github.com/chengxuphd/phantom-gains)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[ReplayLens: Auditing Agents' Use of Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34177 "ReplayLens: Auditing Agents' Use of Outcomes (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A black-box audit of what an agent takes from a log of past actions and their scores: it rebuilds the memory from the log with one relationship changed (scores swapped between actions, intact action–score pairs moved to other record slots, actions renamed in both log and menu, or names moved over unchanged scores) and measures how many later decisions change and what that does to externally scored utility (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026replaylens "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.34177) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34177)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/placebo">placebo</a></span>
- **[Towards Reliable LLM Evaluation: Correcting the Winner's Curse in Adaptive Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05973 "Towards Reliable LLM Evaluation: Correcting the Winner's Curse in Adaptive Benchmarking (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A reporting protocol for scores after prompt or program search. Its target is the fresh-data performance of the whole tune-then-deploy procedure at each tuning budget, not the winning artifact's score (§2). It freezes the shortlist the search returned and, on a separate evaluation pool, repeatedly splits the items into a scoring part that sets smooth selection weights (softmax by default, §3.3, §4) and a held-out part that scores the result, with an item-level Gaussian multiplier bootstrap for intervals and simultaneous bands over budgets (abstract; §3.1–3.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/xu2026siren "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.05973) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05973) · [Code](https://github.com/jefferyz001/siren)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a></span>
- **[ExSPIN](https://www.mdpi.com/1099-4300/27/3/235/pdf "ExSPIN: Explicit Feedback-Based Self-Play Fine-Tuning for Text-to-SQL Parsing (2025)")**: Explicit Feedback-Based Self-Play Fine-Tuning for Text-to-SQL Parsing (*Entropy* 2025). Self-play fine-tuning (SPIN) for text-to-SQL, with the execution results of predicted SQL fed into the parameter update (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yan2025exspin "Summary") · [PDF](https://www.mdpi.com/1099-4300/27/3/235/pdf) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.3390/e27030235)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a></span>
- **[SkillGLoW (Global–Local Weave)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.02217 "SkillGLoW: Procedural-Family Skill Consolidation for Self-Improving Agents on Long-Horizon Task Streams (2026)")**: SkillGLoW: Procedural-Family Skill Consolidation for Self-Improving Agents on Long-Horizon Task Streams (preprint 2026). Organizes the skills a self-improving agent writes for itself by procedural family: the local skill each task writes from its own runs is clustered with others by how the task is solved, each cluster is compressed into one de-instantiated prior, and at solving time a recalled prior is combined with a local skill regenerated from the current task's own feedback (abstract; §3.1–3.5). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yan2026skillglow "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.02217) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02217)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[Balsa](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2201.01441 "Balsa: Learning a Query Optimizer Without Expert Demonstrations (2022)")**: Learning a Query Optimizer Without Expert Demonstrations (SIGMOD 2022). A learned query optimizer trained by RL without expert demonstrations. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2022balsa "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2201.01441) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.01441) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3514221.3517885) · [Code](https://github.com/balsa-project/balsa)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/qo-learned">qo-learned</a></span>

An open toolkit that extracts proof data from Lean and lets programs interact with it, plus ReProver, a retrieval-augmented prover (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2023leandojo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.15626) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.15626) · [Code: LeanDojo](https://github.com/lean-dojo/LeanDojo) · [Code: ReProver](https://github.com/lean-dojo/ReProver)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>

- **[Input Reduction Enhanced LLM-based Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.15251 "Input Reduction Enhanced LLM-based Program Repair (2025)")** (ICSE 2026 (per the PDF's running header), 2025). LLM program repair when the failing test input is long: an LLM writes, once per task, a delta-debugging (ddmin) script that shrinks the failure-inducing input while the buggy program and a correct reference still print different outputs, and the repair prompt carries the reduced input instead of the full one (abstract; §3.1–3.4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2025reducefix "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.15251) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15251)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/longctx">longctx</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/reduce">reduce</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[SQLDriller](#/papers/yang2025sqldriller "Automated Validating and Fixing of Text-to-SQL Translation with Execution Consistency (2025)")**: Automated Validating and Fixing of Text-to-SQL Translation with Execution Consistency (PACMMOD / SIGMOD 2025). Finds and fixes wrong NL→SQL mappings in training data via counterexample databases and "execution consistency". <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2025sqldriller "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3725271)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-select">nl2dsl-select</a></span>
- **[Self-Evolving Code-with-Image Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.11292 "Self-Evolving Code-with-Image Reasoning (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Visual questions whose exact answers need a multi-step computation over the pixels, solved by a frozen multimodal model given only a Python interpreter ("Code-with-Image"), on the authors' CwI-Bench of generated task families with labels correct by construction and disjoint train, validation and test splits (abstract; §3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026codewithimage "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.11292) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11292)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>

A monitor for reward hacking in self-evolving loops that attaches through two black-box hooks (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026hackprobe "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.04665) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04665)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>
- **[Better Harnesses, Smaller Models: Building 90\% Cheaper Agents via Automated Harness Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.08938 "Better Harnesses, Smaller Models: Building 90\% Cheaper Agents via Automated Harness Adaptation (2026)")** (Smaller Models"), preprint 2026). A frontier meta-agent (`gemini-3.1-pro-preview`) adapts the harness of a small open-weight model per task, so that it approaches a frontier model with a generic harness at a fraction of the cost; seven business-style agentic tasks, three small models (abstract; §IV-A; §IV-B). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026harnessadapt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.08938) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08938) · [Code](https://github.com/malusamayo/slm-harness-adaptation)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[How Sensitive Are LLM Leaderboard Claims to Hidden Model Selection?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28177 "How Sensitive Are LLM Leaderboard Claims to Hidden Model Selection? (2026)")** (preprint 2026 (v2)). Asks how many privately scored sibling variants a published leaderboard margin over a fixed comparator can survive: under a Gaussian margin model it derives the least-favorable null of the selected margin and inverts it into a per-claim sensitivity curve, the largest hidden count k̄ as a function of a lower bound on the siblings' correlation (abstract; §1; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026leaderboard "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28177) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28177)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[SkillOpt](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23904 "SkillOpt: Executive Strategy for Self-Evolving Agent Skills (2026)")**: Executive Strategy for Self-Evolving Agent Skills (preprint 2026). Trains an agent's skill document like weights: bounded add/delete/replace edits accepted only on validation gains. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026skillopt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23904) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23904)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[When Agent Memory Needs to Compute: Evidence Access and Executable Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19474 "When Agent Memory Needs to Compute: Evidence Access and Executable Aggregation (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Answers questions about an agent's logged history that combine many events (e.g. how often an action was taken): Trajectory Memory (TM) keeps the trajectory verbatim and shows a budgeted, order-preserving view chosen at question time, and TallyMem adds an event ledger of every step, over which a model-written event specification (scope, event predicate, operation) is run by a deterministic executor whose result and supporting step IDs join TM's view (abstract; §4; §6). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yang2026tallymem "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19474) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19474)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/longctx">longctx</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a></span>

Extends textual gradients to pipelines of several LLM calls and functional nodes, including cyclic ones (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yin2025llmautodiff "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.16673) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16673) · [Code](https://github.com/SylphAI-Inc/AdalFlow)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[CaLIF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19525 "Diagnosing and Correcting Reward Hacking in Verifiable Instruction Following (2026)")**: Diagnosing and Correcting Reward Hacking in Verifiable Instruction Following (preprint 2026). Verifiable instruction-following benchmarks such as IFBench check only the output constraints appended to a request, by program; the authors show that frontier models can receive full credit for responses with little answer content, and that RL with the verifier as reward raises constraint satisfaction while lowering LLM-judged response quality: in all three released OLMo 3 DPO–RLVR checkpoint pairs, whose RL includes this task among others, and in their own IF-RLVR runs (abstract; §1; §3; §5.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yoon2026calif "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19525) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19525)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>

Agentic migration of Oracle stored procedures to PostgreSQL: translate, compile, execute, diff results, repair. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yu2026agentmigrate "Summary") · [PDF](https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3810991.3811630) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3810991.3811630)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/difftest">difftest</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.00520 "SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark (2026)")** (ICML 2026 (per the authors' repo)). Strengthens SWE-Bench test suites in two stages: tests aimed at patch-relevant lines found by program slicing, then LLM-made mutants of the gold patch that pass the existing tests, with new tests written to reject them (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yu2026sweabs "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.00520) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00520) · [Code](https://github.com/OpenAgentEval/SWE-ABS)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[FORGE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.15209 "Failure-Guided Co-Evolution of Prompts and Training Data (2026)")**: Failure-Guided Co-Evolution of Prompts and Training Data (preprint 2026). Co-evolves the prompts and the training data of an LM program whose model weights are frozen (§ "Problem Formulation"; the paper numbers no main-text sections). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yuan2026forge "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.15209) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15209)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[TextGrad](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.07496 "TextGrad: Automatic 'Differentiation' via Text (2024)")**: Automatic "Differentiation" via Text (preprint 2024). Backpropagates natural-language feedback through a computation graph of LLM calls and tools. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yuksekgonul2024textgrad "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.07496) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07496) · [Code](https://github.com/zou-group/textgrad)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[ExPairT-LLM](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.10855 "ExPairT-LLM: Exact Learning for LLM Code Selection by Pairwise Queries (2026)")**: Exact Learning for LLM Code Selection by Pairwise Queries (AAAI 2026 (per the PDF's copyright line, p. 1)). Code selection as exact learning with an LLM as the oracle: candidate programs are clustered by their outputs on a few inputs, a round-robin tournament of "pairwise membership" queries (which of two output lists better fits the task) picks a cluster, and "pairwise equivalence" queries ask the LLM for an input on which two programs of that cluster differ; an input confirmed by running both programs splits the cluster and the loop repeats (abstract; § "Introduction"; § "Exact Learning by Pairwise Queries to LLMs", Alg. 1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/yuviler2025expairt "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.10855) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10855) · [Code](https://github.com/TomYuviler/ExPairT-LLM)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
- **[ELT-Bench-Verified](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29399 "ELT-Bench-Verified: Benchmark Quality Issues Underestimate AI Agent Capabilities (2026)")**: Benchmark Quality Issues Underestimate AI Agent Capabilities (PVLDB 20(1) (reference-format block; not yet published), 2026). Re-evaluates ELT-Bench with a newer model ("a single model upgrade", §2.1) and audits its quality (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zanoli2026eltverified "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29399) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29399) · [Code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/ELT-Bench)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/dbtask">dbtask</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[STaR (Self-Taught Reasoner)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.14465 "STaR: Bootstrapping Reasoning With Reasoning (2022)")**: STaR: Bootstrapping Reasoning With Reasoning (NeurIPS 2022). A few-shot-prompted LLM writes rationales for many questions; only those whose final answer matches the dataset's answer are kept, the model is fine-tuned on them, and the loop repeats (abstract; §3.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zelikman2022star "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.14465) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.14465) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/068431-1126) · [Code](https://github.com/ezelikman/STaR)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a></span>

An LLM judges semantic and "weak" semantic equivalence for text-to-SQL. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zeng2025taming "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.09359) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09359)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>

- **[LPR: Large Language Models-Aided Program Reduction](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.13064 "LPR: Large Language Models-Aided Program Reduction (2024)")** (ISSTA 2024). Shrinks programs that trigger compiler bugs, in C, Rust and JavaScript, to smaller ones that still trigger the bug, by having an LLM make the language-specific transformations that a language-generic reducer can't (abstract, §1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2023lpr "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.13064) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.13064) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3650212.3652126)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/reduce">reduce</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a></span>
