Searches the workflow itself: code-represented graphs of LLM-calling nodes (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2024aflow "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.10762) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10762) · [Code](https://github.com/FoundationAgents/AFlow)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Generative Verifiers (GenRM)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.15240 "Generative Verifiers: Reward Modeling as Next-Token Prediction (2025)")**: Generative Verifiers: Reward Modeling as Next-Token Prediction (ICLR 2025). Reward modeling as next-token prediction: the verifier answers "Yes"/"No" (§3.1), in its CoT variant after writing a rationale (§3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2024genrm "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2408.15240) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15240)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>

Contexts as evolving playbooks, built by a generator, reflector and curator. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025ace "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.04618) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04618) · [Code](https://github.com/ace-agent/ace)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>

A coding agent edits its own code; each new agent is scored on coding benchmarks and archived if it still compiles and can edit code, so the Gödel machine's proof that a change helps is replaced by empirical validation (abstract; §3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025dgm "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.22954) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22954) · [Code](https://github.com/jennyzzt/dgm)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>

A manual audit of sampled question–answer pairs from 16 knowledge-graph question-answering (KGQA) benchmarks, WebQSP and CWQ among them, for wrong, outdated or incomplete answers and ambiguous, trivial or unanswerable questions (§3.1, Tab. 1; App. B.3); then KGQAGen, a pipeline that writes questions over Wikidata, the 10,787-question KGQAGen-10k built with it (§5), and an evaluation of LLMs and KG-RAG systems on it (§6, Tab. 2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025kgqagen "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.23495) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23495) · [Code](https://github.com/liangliang6v6/KGQAGen)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sparql">dsl-sparql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[RLM (Recursive Language Models)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.24601 "Recursive Language Models (2025)")**: Recursive Language Models (preprint 2025). An inference scaffold that stores a long prompt as a variable in a Python REPL and lets the model inspect it with code and call itself, or a smaller model, on slices it builds in loops; only short previews of each step's output return to the model (abstract; §2, Alg. 1; smaller sub-call model: §3.2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025rlm "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.24601) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24601) · [Code](https://github.com/alexzhang13/rlm)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/offload">offload</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[SPFT-SQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.03937 "SPFT-SQL: Enhancing Large Language Model for Text-to-SQL Parsing by Self-Play Fine-Tuning (2025)")**: Enhancing Large Language Model for Text-to-SQL Parsing by Self-Play Fine-Tuning (Findings of EMNLP 2025). Self-play fine-tuning for text-to-SQL, with an error-driven loss on the opponent model's incorrect SQL (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2025spftsql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.03937) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03937) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-emnlp.59)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a></span>
- **[Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14585 "Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip: Diagnosing When It Helps in Compound AI Systems (2026)")**: Diagnosing When It Helps in Compound AI Systems (CTB@ICML 2026 (workshop)). Runs six optimizers on four single-agent tasks, 3 repeats, two executor models (§4, §4.1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026coinflip "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.14585) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14585)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-evolve">promptopt-evolve</a></span>
- **[Positional Failures in Long-Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23170 "Positional Failures in Long-Context LLMs: A Blind Spot in Reasoning Benchmarks (2026)")**: A Blind Spot in Reasoning Benchmarks ((CRE), preprint 2026). Audits 11 long-context benchmarks for whether they control where the task sits in a long input (App. F, Tab. 22), and the official sources of four recent flagship releases for whether position-controlled benchmarks appear in their main result tables (App. G, Tab. 23); then moves a GSM8K question from the end to the middle of an 8K, 32K or 64K-token context of filler of three kinds, for nine API models, with ARC-Challenge questions at 8K only (§3, §4.4, Tab. 3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026positional "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23170) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23170)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/longctx">longctx</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Quarry ("Planning to Hammer")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.17981 "Planning to Hammer: Difficulty-Aware Decomposition for Automating Rocq Proofs (2026)")**: Planning to Hammer: Difficulty-Aware Decomposition for Automating Rocq Proofs (preprint 2026 (submitted to OOPSLA 2026)). The LLM proposes decompositions of a Rocq goal into sublemmas, and CoqHammer discharges the pieces (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026quarry "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.17981) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17981) · [Code](https://github.com/ningZhang-cs/QUARRY)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/itp">itp</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a></span>
- **[Ratchet: How Reliable Must an LLM Judge Be to Retire a Skill?](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22148 "Ratchet: How Reliable Must an LLM Judge Be to Retire a Skill? (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A skill library kept by one frozen LLM in five roles over an append-only log: a router picks at most one skill per task, a critic labels failures, a synthesizer writes skills from clustered failure patterns, and a curator retires a skill once its measured contribution (passes minus failures over its routed trials) falls to −τ after enough trials and caps the active library at a fixed width (§3); the authors state a floor on how far below the no-skill control a governed library can fall, finite because of the width and τ (§4, Prop. 1; as printed it fails for skills with fewer than Nmin trials, and their own statistic doesn't meet its unbiasedness assumption even under unit tests). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026ratchet "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22148) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22148) · [Code](https://github.com/amazon-science/Self-Evolving-Agents-Ratchet)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[SLER](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.04169 "Efficient Query Rewrite Rule Discovery via Standardized Enumeration and Learning-to-Rank (2026)")**: Efficient Query Rewrite Rule Discovery via Standardized Enumeration and Learning-to-Rank (preprint 2026). Rule discovery with standardized plan templates and a learning-to-rank pre-filter, scaling to larger plans. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026sler "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.04169) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04169)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairgen-apply">pairgen-apply</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-discover">rules-discover</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rules-verify">rules-verify</a></span>
- **[Who Verifies the Verifier? Co-Evolving Inspectable Graders with Self-Improving Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.11464 "Who Verifies the Verifier? Co-Evolving Inspectable Graders with Self-Improving Agents (2026)")** (NeurIPS 2026 Workshop "Who Verifies the Agents? Toward Reliable Agent Development" (PDF p. 1 footer)). Evolves the grader of a self-improving agent rather than the agent: a readable expression (any-of, all-of, votes) over small, mostly deterministic drawback detectors (static checks, execution in a sandbox or a live warehouse, narrow LLM-judge questions), selected for agreement with a ten-item anchored reference set and with detector consensus on unlabeled outputs, "never for the agent's score"; paired with the Ratchet skill loop ([Ratchet](#/papers/zhang2026ratchet "Ratchet: How Reliable Must an LLM Judge Be to Retire a Skill? (2026)")) as Double Ratchet (abstract; §3.1–3.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhang2026whoverifies "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.11464) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11464)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[ExpeL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.10144 "ExpeL: LLM Agents Are Experiential Learners (2024)")**: LLM Agents Are Experiential Learners (AAAI 2024). An agent gathers experience on training tasks by retrying them with Reflexion, then at test time adds natural-language insights drawn from that experience, and retrieved successful trajectories, to its ReAct prompt, without weight updates (§1, §4.1–§4.3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2023expel "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.10144) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.10144) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v38i17.29936) · [Code](https://github.com/LeapLabTHU/ExpeL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[LLM-SQL-Solver](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.10321 "LLM-SQL-Solver: Can LLMs Determine SQL Equivalence? (2025)")**: Can LLMs Determine SQL Equivalence? (IEEE BigData 2025). Asks whether LLMs can judge semantic and relaxed SQL equivalence. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2023llmsqlsolver "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.10321) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.10321) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/BigData66926.2025.11401595) · [Code](https://github.com/ZhaoFuheng/LLM-SQL-Solver)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[Absolute Zero](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03335 "Absolute Zero: Reinforced Self-play Reasoning with Zero Data (2025)")**: Reinforced Self-play Reasoning with Zero Data (preprint 2025). One model proposes code-reasoning tasks and solves them; a code executor checks both. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2025absolutezero "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03335) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03335) · [Code](https://github.com/LeapLabTHU/Absolute-Zero-Reasoner)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/transfer">transfer</a></span>
- **[Polygon](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.06542 "Polygon: Symbolic Reasoning for SQL using Conflict-Driven Under-Approximation Search (2025)")**: Symbolic Reasoning for SQL using Conflict-Driven Under-Approximation Search (PLDI 2025). Generates an input database on which several queries' outputs satisfy a property, e.g. two queries differ. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhao2025polygon "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.06542) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06542) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3729303) · [Code: polygon-artifact-evaluation](https://zenodo.org/records/15059866) · [Code: polygon-sql](https://github.com/polygon-sql/polygon)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/bounded">bounded</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10969 "Engineering Reliable Commit Gates for Agentic AI: Cost-Aware Verification Portfolios under Common-Mode Data Failures (2026)")** (preprint 2026). A gate between an agent's proposed state-changing action and its execution chooses a verification plan (re-check on the same source, another verifier model, an independent read, a transactional guard, deferral to a person) from metadata a deployment can observe, calibrated to a risk target (abstract; §III). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zheng2026vpcontrol "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.10969) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10969) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1) · [Code](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33511441.v1)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[Semantic Evaluation for Text-to-SQL with Distilled Test Suites](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.02840 "Semantic Evaluation for Text-to-SQL with Distilled Test Suites (2020)")** (EMNLP 2020). "Test-suite accuracy": a small suite of databases distilled to separate the gold query from near-miss neighbours. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhong2020testsuite "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.02840) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.02840) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2020.emnlp-main.29) · [Code](https://github.com/taoyds/test-suite-sql-eval)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/cex-search">cex-search</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-eval">nl2dsl-eval</a></span>
- **[EQUITAS](#/papers/zhou2019equitas "Automated verification of query equivalence using satisfiability modulo theories (2019)")**: Automated verification of query equivalence using satisfiability modulo theories (PVLDB 12(11) 2019). SMT-based prover: a query becomes a symbolic representation (SR) ⟨COND, COLS, ASSIGN⟩ over symbolic tuples, and containment is checked with Z3. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2019equitas "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3342263.3342267)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[SPES](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2004.00481 "SPES: A Symbolic Approach to Proving Query Equivalence Under Bag Semantics (2022)")**: A Symbolic Approach to Proving Query Equivalence Under Bag Semantics (ICDE 2022). Proves equivalence under **bag** semantics symbolically. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2020spes "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2004.00481) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.00481) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1109/icde53745.2022.00250) · [Code](https://github.com/georgia-tech-db/spes)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/smt">smt</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a></span>
- **[LearnedRewrite](#/papers/zhou2021learnedrewrite "A learned query rewrite system using Monte Carlo tree search (2021)")**: A learned query rewrite system using Monte Carlo tree search (PVLDB 15(1) 2022, 2021). Chooses the *order* of Calcite rewrite rules by Monte Carlo tree search. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2021learnedrewrite "Summary") · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.14778/3485450.3485456) · [Code](https://github.com/XuanheZhou/LearnedRewrite)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/pairgen-apply">pairgen-apply</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/qo-learned">qo-learned</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/rewrite-classic">rewrite-classic</a></span>
- **[APE](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.01910 "Large Language Models Are Human-Level Prompt Engineers (2023)")**: Large Language Models Are Human-Level Prompt Engineers (ICLR 2023). An LLM proposes instructions from input–output examples; the best-scoring one is kept. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2022ape "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.01910) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.01910) · [Code](https://github.com/keirp/automatic_prompt_engineer)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>
- **[Self-Discover](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03620 "SELF-DISCOVER: Large Language Models Self-Compose Reasoning Structures (2024)")**: SELF-DISCOVER: Large Language Models Self-Compose Reasoning Structures (NeurIPS 2024). The model selects, adapts and composes reasoning modules into an explicit reasoning structure once per task, then follows it on every instance of the task (abstract; §2). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2024selfdiscover "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.03620) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03620) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.52202/079017-4004)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a></span>
- **[CrackSQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.00882 "Cracking SQL Barriers: An LLM-based Dialect Translation System (2025)")**: Cracking SQL Barriers: An LLM-based Dialect Translation System (PACMMOD / SIGMOD 2025). Hybrid dialect translation: rules where they apply, LLMs for the rest, with segmentation of long queries. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2025cracksql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.00882) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00882) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3725278) · [Code](https://github.com/OpenDataBox/CrackSQL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dialect-translate">dialect-translate</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a></span>
- **[Many Steps into One: Reasoning Contraction and the Limits of Safe Memory Reuse for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17544 "Many Steps into One: Reasoning Contraction and the Limits of Safe Memory Reuse for LLM Agents (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Treats thought templates, skill libraries, agent memories and caches as one-step memories of multi-step derivations (each a "contracted memory") and trusting and retiring them as sequential tests; proves that clipped counters are change detectors testing a change nobody chose and that unclipped ones may never retire a partially stale memory ("vote counters (ExpeL, ACE) are change detectors tuned to the wrong change", §8), and gives a limit, in the worst case and as δ → 0, on how much reasoning any safe memory can skip when facts change and each memory is judged only by its own verified outcomes (abstract; §1; §3–4, Thm. 1, Thm. 3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026contraction "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17544) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17544)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a></span>
- **[More Convincing, Not More Correct](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.05904 "More Convincing, Not More Correct: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (2026)")**: Self-Play Reward Hacking of Reference-Free LLM Judges (preprint 2026). Self-play against a reference-free LLM judge: one model answers GSM8K questions, judges its own answers without a reference, and is trained by DPO on the judge's accept/reject pairs, while a hidden anchor, an exact-match check on the final answer that the judge never sees, audits true accuracy (abstract; §3; §5). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026convincing "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.05904) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05904)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/math">math</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[The Verifier is the Curriculum](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09709 "The Verifier is the Curriculum: Precision Sets the Return on Search in Code Self-Distillation (2026)")**: Precision Sets the Return on Search in Code Self-Distillation (preprint 2026). Rejection-sampling self-distillation of a code generator, gated by whether the generated game project launches cleanly, with no judge (abstract). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026curriculum "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.09709) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09709)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
- **[SALUS](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05540 "SALUS: Automated Auditing of NL-to-SQL Benchmarks through Weak Supervision of Multi-Agent Output (2026)")**: Automated Auditing of NL-to-SQL Benchmarks through Weak Supervision of Multi-Agent Output (SIGMOD 2027 (PACMMOD 4(6)), 2026). Flags wrong gold SQL in a text-to-SQL benchmark without human labels: four LLM agents (in the primary configuration GPT-5, GPT-4o, Claude Opus 4.6 and Gemini 2.5 Pro; §5.1) each write one query per task; 44 labeling functions over execution results, ASTs and question cues vote on the gold query; a Snorkel label model turns the votes into weak labels, and per-agent reliability models over 12 features of the gold SQL weight each agent's verdict (abstract; §3; §5.1, Tab. 3). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhou2026salus "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.05540) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05540) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3856369) · [Code](https://github.com/ucisharadlab/SALUS)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/compact">compact</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/judge">judge</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/stats">stats</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-assist">llm-assist</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a></span>
- **[ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM-Generated Optimization Models](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.29431 "ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM-Generated Optimization Models (2026)")** (preprint 2026). Grades an LLM-generated optimization model against a reference not with one equivalent/not-equivalent verdict but with a profile of seven relations, E0–E6: it builds and ingests, a verified variable map, feasible sets (same space or under an affine lift), objective order, optimal value, and optimizer sets (abstract; §1; Tab. 1). The authors say it is an evaluator, "not a new benchmark dataset": the problems come from Bench4Opt (§1). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhu2026modelequivbench "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.29431) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29431)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/cex">cex</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/labels">labels</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/prove">prove</a></span>
- **[ReViSQL](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.20004 "Human-Level Text-to-SQL via Reinforcement Learning on Verified Data, Without Pipeline Engineering (2026)")**: Human-Level Text-to-SQL via Reinforcement Learning on Verified Data, Without Pipeline Engineering (preprint, 2026). RLVR text-to-SQL trained on BIRD-Platinum, a re-verified slice of BIRD Train. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhu2026revisql "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.20004) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20004) · [Code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/ReViSQL)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/hacking">hacking</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/scaling">scaling</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/dsl-sql">dsl-sql</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/llm-gen">llm-gen</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/nl2dsl-data">nl2dsl-data</a></span>
- **[SSL ("Socratic Skill Learning")](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17860 "Socratic Skill Learning: Interleaving Teaching and Practice for Transferable Agent Skills (2026)")**: Socratic Skill Learning: Interleaving Teaching and Practice for Transferable Agent Skills (preprint 2026). A Student model practises on one anchor task and writes an agent skill package itself while an Educator model reviews its work and the live package read-only and explains, challenges, diagnoses or stays silent; fresh solver sessions then use the frozen package on held-out members of the task's family, in the authors' SkillTransferBench (35 task families, 408 executable cases with private tests, six domains) (abstract; §3–4). <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/zhu2026socratic "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17860) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17860) · [Code](https://github.com/NJU-LINK/Socratic-Skill-Learning)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/agent">agent</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/benchmark">benchmark</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/code">code</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/harness">harness</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-context">promptopt-context</a><a class="tag sub" href="#/tags/promptopt-feedback">promptopt-feedback</a></span>
- **[mmGRPO](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.04660 "Composing Policy Gradients and Prompt Optimization for Language Model Programs (2026)")**: Composing Policy Gradients and Prompt Optimization for Language Model Programs (ACM CAIS 2026). GRPO for multi-module LM programs, composed with prompt optimization. <span class="links">[🔎](#/papers/ziems2025mmgrpo "Summary") · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.04660) · [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04660) · [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/3786335.3813164)</span> <span class="tags"><a class="tag" href="#/tags/llm">llm</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/method">method</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/nl">nl</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/promptopt">promptopt</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/rlvr">rlvr</a><a class="tag" href="#/tags/study">study</a></span>
